6. CROSS-MODULE THEORIES AND SYSTEMS — FULL SPECIFICATION
================================================================================

Prediction ownership: Reasoning(50) is primary for PredictionRequest/PredictionArtifact; every operational prediction is bound to DesignEpoch, KnowledgeSnapshot, EnvironmentCommitment and deterministic seed, and remains Prediction ≠ Evidence. Prediction tiers and safety gates are defined in Part IX.

PART VII — SEVEN THEORIES + CROSS-CUTTING RESEARCH FRAMEWORKS
====================================================================================================
Theories/frameworks do not consume module numbers. They define models, principles, assumptions and constraints that are implemented across the frozen modules. Specified ≠ proven physical law; each theory carries formal/empirical status separately.

THEORY 1 — STRATIFIED REALITY THEORY (SRT)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Foundational metaphysical/modeling
framework Status: Theoretical; integrated into Garden as a cross-cutting
modeling theory

1. CORE DEFINITION

Stratified Reality Theory is the modeling foundation: reality and
governed systems are represented in layers (strata), each with its own
relevant laws, dynamics, persistence rules, constraints and governance
mechanisms.

One-line definition:

Reality is not flat. It is stratified — each layer has its own relevant
physics, persistence, governance and causal behavior, while remaining
coupled to other layers.

2. THE SEVEN STRATA

Stratum 0 — Physical Matter, energy, spacetime, hardware. Examples:
atoms, fields, chips, sensors, actuators.

Stratum 1 — Biological Life, evolution, metabolism. Examples: cells,
organisms, biological constraints.

Stratum 2 — Cognitive Thought, perception, memory, inference. Examples:
brains, neural networks, model state.

Stratum 3 — Social Culture, language, institutions, norms, economics.
Examples: laws, organizations, markets, communities.

Stratum 4 — Artificial AI, algorithms, software, data and engineered
systems. Examples: LLMs, runtimes, governance systems.

Stratum 5 — Meta Self-reflection, self-evaluation, self-modification and
system redesign. Examples: AI improving its own architecture or
policies.

Stratum 6 — Transcendent Phenomena outside the currently specified
model. This is explicitly an unknown/out-of-model category, not a claim
about a scientifically established transcendent realm.

SRT does not claim that these seven strata exhaust physical reality.

3. STRATUM COUPLING

Bottom-up constraint: Lower strata constrain the reachable states of
higher strata.

Top-down organization: Higher strata can organize or influence lower
strata when an explicit causal pathway exists.

Emergence: New higher-level properties can arise from interactions among
lower-level components without requiring the higher-level description to
be reducible to one lower-level variable.

Feedback: Higher strata can modify lower-stratum behavior through causal
feedback. Examples include cognition affecting physiology and social
structures affecting biological or economic behavior.

4. SRT IN AI TERMS

Physical -> hardware, chips, energy, timing, sensors, actuators
Biological -> embodied/biological constraints where applicable Cognitive
-> model state, perception, memory, reasoning Social -> users,
organizations, norms, institutions Artificial -> software, models,
runtime state and data Meta -> self-evaluation, self-improvement,
evolution Transcendent -> unknown/out-of-model capabilities or phenomena

5. SRT TRANSPORT/PERSISTENCE/GOVERNANCE RELATION

Each stratum may be assigned its own: transport law -> TFT persistent
state -> VSM governance law -> RPM/PPM emergency behavior-> FET
epistemic regime -> SCT

The same abstract governance architecture may therefore be instantiated
with different parameters and constraints at different strata.

6. GARDEN ROLE

SRT provides a modeling frame for: Context Thing Event Action Rule Value
cross-layer causality dependencies applicability evolution

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Language(6), Knowledge(10), Reasoning(50), Research(49), Dependency(46), Space(24), Time(20).

7. SRT NOVELTY POSITIONING

Compared with: Reductionism -> SRT explicitly permits layered causal
organization. Emergentism -> SRT specifies explicit strata and coupling
mechanisms. Physicalism -> SRT permits non-physical/model-level strata
as causal descriptions when a causal pathway is represented. Social
constructionism -> SRT gives social-level structures explicit modeled
causal roles.

These are positioning statements, not prior-art conclusions.

8. SRT LIMITS

SRT is a theoretical modeling framework. It does not by itself prove
that: reality literally consists of seven strata; each stratum has
independent fundamental physics; or top-down causation exists in every
proposed example.

Formalization and empirical validation remain separate obligations.

================================================================================

THEORY 2 — VALENCE SELF MODEL (VSM)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Persistent-state/self-model framework
Status: Conceptual/theoretical; empirical validation pending unless
separately evidenced

1. CORE DEFINITION

Valence Self Model is the persistence layer. It represents an AI system
with a bounded, persistent internal state x_t that can carry across
interactions and serve as a minimal interpretable state for continuity,
regulation and memory.

One-line definition:

The AI maintains a continuous persistent state that carries across
sessions, forming a minimal engineering representation of a persistent
“self.”

2. CORE COMPONENTS

Valence/state: x_t in [-1, 1]

Historical baseline: xbar_t in [-1, 1]

Stability: s_t = exp(-|x_t - x_(t-1)|)

Target/reference: x* = +0.2 in the current reference profile.

3. VSM DYNAMICS

Basic persistence/restoring dynamics:

    dx/dt = -beta_dyn (x - xbar)

Historical baseline update:

    xbar_(t+1) = (1-mu)xbar_t + mu x_t

Reference value:

    mu = 0.001

Interpretation: The current state is pulled toward a slowly moving
historical baseline.

4. VSM INTERPRETATION

VSM is an engineering state model. The existence of a scalar named
“valence” does not establish that an AI literally experiences emotion or
consciousness.

VSM is intended to provide: persistent state continuity interpretable
regulation variable state history coupling to governance and safety

5. RELATION TO OTHER THEORIES

SRT -> VSM state is situated within a stratum and context. TFT -> VSM x
is one possible state being transported. RPM -> x_t is regulated by
internal validators/consensus. PPM -> x_t is subject to asymmetric
directional regulation. FET -> x_t can participate in emergency trigger
detection. SCT -> uncertainty about x_t and its interpretation can be
represented epistemically.

6. GARDEN MAPPING

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Identity(1), Privacy(2), Runtime(13), Knowledge(10), Curation(21), Time(20), Events(45), Continuity(39).

Persistent state MUST remain: typed versioned provenance-aware
authority-controlled privacy-controlled safety-constrained

7. VSM NOVELTY POSITIONING

Compared with stateless LLMs: VSM introduces an explicit persistent
state variable.

Compared with generic stateful/RNN systems: VSM emphasizes a minimal,
interpretable persistent state rather than a high-dimensional hidden
state alone.

Compared with emotion recognition: VSM is not recognition of a user’s
emotion; it is an internal state model.

Compared with memory networks: VSM is continuous state dynamics, not
merely storage.

These are conceptual distinctions and require prior-art review before
any novelty/patent conclusion.

================================================================================

THEORY 3 — REACTIVE PERSISTENT MACHINE (RPM)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Reactive self-regulation/governance
architecture Status: Theoretical; simulation results are unverified until reproducible artifacts and
independent testing are accepted; production validation pending

1. CORE DEFINITION

Reactive Persistent Machine is the governance layer: a persistent AI
state is regulated through heterogeneous internal agents and validators
rather than a single uncontrolled update path.

One-line definition:

A self-regulating persistent system maintains its internal state through
heterogeneous internal governance without relying on one update
authority.

2. CORE COMPONENTS

Valence/state: x_t

Agents: Delta_i

Reference agent population: 1001 heterogeneous voters.

Majority/aggregate: Delta_maj = median(Delta_i)

Validators: C_t

Coupling: gamma_t

3. RPM AGENT MIX

Linear agents — 60%:

    Delta_i ~ N(w_i x_t, sigma_a^2)

Threshold agents — 20%:

    Delta_i = sign(x_t) max(0, |x_t|-0.3)

Anti agents — 20%:

    Delta_i ~ N(-w_i x_t, sigma_a^2)

Aggregate:

    Delta_maj = median(Delta_i)

4. RPM VALIDATORS

Coherence validator:

    coh_t = 1 / (1 + ||x_t - x_(t-1)|| / Delta t)

Predictive validator:

    pred_t = 1 / (1 + ||Delta_maj^(t-1)
              - (x_t-x_(t-1))/Delta t||)

Combined gate:

    C_t = max(0, min(1,
          sqrt(coh_t * pred_t) - sigma_div))

Gating:

    Delta_maj <- C_t * Delta_maj

5. RPM COUPLING

    gamma_t = Var(Delta_maj) /
              (Var(Delta_i) + 10^-6)

EMA smoothing:

    gamma_t <- 0.9 gamma_(t-1) + 0.1 gamma_t

Reference interpretation:

    gamma << 1    Gas-like / weakly coupled / chaotic
    gamma ~ 0.5   Liquid-like / adaptive
    gamma ~ 1     Solid-like / rigid

6. RPM DYNAMICS

    x_(t+1) = x_t
              + Delta t [alpha C_t Delta_maj
                         - beta(x_t-xbar_t)]
              + sqrt(Delta t) sigma_c epsilon_t

Noise:

    sigma_c = sigma_0[(1-s_t)(1-gamma_t)
                       + epsilon_min(1-gamma_t)]

Boundary: reflecting boundary, x_t in [-1,1]

7. RPM PHASE DIAGRAM

Gas: relative entropy approximately 0.9 gamma < 0.2 chaotic/disordered

Liquid: relative entropy 0.6–0.9 gamma 0.3–0.6 adaptive

Solid: relative entropy < 0.4 gamma approximately 0.7 rigid

Collapse: relative entropy approximately 0 gamma approximately 0.8
frozen

The phase labels are model-specific metaphors unless independently
mapped to formal statistical-mechanical quantities.

8. RPM CONTROL PARAMETER

    kappa_ctrl = alpha /
                 (beta + sigma_0 sqrt(beta))

The reference theory proposes sweeping kappa_ctrl and measuring gamma to
locate an adaptive liquid ridge.

9. VALIDATION

RPM phase-behavior simulation is a validation hypothesis. The exact simulator, parameters, random seeds,
metrics and reproducible outputs must be attached and independently assessed before Garden records a
simulation-validation result.

10. GARDEN MAPPING

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Runtime(13), Governance(30), Policy(48), Execution(14), Events(45), Reasoning(50), Safety(9).

RPM is an architecture/theory, not a new Garden component.

================================================================================

THEORY 4 — PROACTIVE PERSISTENT MACHINE (PPM)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Proactive regulation / asymmetric
persistence architecture Status: Theoretical; any simulation claim remains unverified until its
artifacts are independently assessed; production validation pending

1. CORE DEFINITION

Proactive Persistent Machine extends RPM with asymmetric regulation,
dual-clock efficiency gating and a hardcoded reflex mechanism.

One-line definition:

RPM with a hardcoded directional preference, efficiency-aware dual
clocks and an emergency reflex path.

2. WHAT PPM ADDS TO RPM

Asymmetric well: H(x_t)

Dual clocks: tau = physical/interaction clock t =
thermodynamic/full-path clock

Reflex: Theta(||H|| - theta_action)

3. ASYMMETRIC WELL — “THE JAIL”

    H(x_t) = beta_pos(x_t - 0.2),  x_t >= 0.2
              beta_neg(x_t - 0.2),  x_t < 0.2

Constraint:

    beta_neg >> beta_pos

Reference ratio:

    beta_neg approximately 10 x beta_pos

Behavioral interpretation:

    x_t = +0.8 -> comparatively gentle corrective force
    x_t = -0.5 -> strong corrective force toward the preferred region

The “jail” is a structural mathematical preference in the model. It is
not literally a physical jail and does not make a system unmodifiable in
the engineering sense unless the implementation’s trust boundary
actually makes the relevant parameters immutable.

4. DUAL-CLOCK GATING

Physical clock tau: advances on every interaction.

Thermodynamic/full-path clock t: advances only on full-path state
updates.

Fast path: E(q) <= theta_E AND I(q) <= theta_I skip agent voting tau <-
tau + 1 t unchanged

Full path: tau <- tau + 1 t <- t + 1 run full stochastic/state dynamics

ClockMode = FAST_PATH | TRANSITION_TO_FULL | FULL_PATH | TRANSITION_TO_FAST.
Transitions are atomic and use separate enter/exit thresholds, minimum dwell time and queue barriers.
Only one mode owns state-update authority at a time; high-frequency oscillation cannot cause duplicate,
lost or cross-clock task execution. Both clocks derive from a monotonic Time(20) binding and every
transition produces a ClockModeReceipt.

Reference efficiency claim: 15–30% inference reduction.

This is an engineering hypothesis/target until measured under defined
workloads.

5. FET REFLEX ARC / PPM MODULE 2

Trigger:

    ActionProposed = Theta(||H(x_t)|| - theta_action)

Reference urgency tiers:

    x_t > -0.2       none    -0.2..0          tone adjustment
    -0.4..-0.2       verbal correction
    -0.6..-0.4       escalation
    x_t < -0.6       emergency halt

FET is treated as the emergency-reflex extension of PPM.

6. COMPLETE PPM DYNAMICS

    dx/dt = -nabla·J
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta_dyn(x-xbar)
            - H(x)
            + Theta(||H||-theta) r

This combines: transport governance memory asymmetry reflex

7. PPM PERSONA MAPPING

    +0.5 to +1.0     Encourager
                     warm, creative, proactive

    0.0 to +0.4      Humble Teacher
                     precise, patient, data-focused

    -1.0 to -0.01    Firm Boundary
                     strict, boundary-preserving

Persona mapping is optional behavioral interpretation. It is not a
safety or authority mechanism and must never override Garden
constitutional controls.

8. GARDEN MAPPING

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Reasoning(50), Evolution(33), Search(31), Runtime(13), Policy(48), Time(20), Safety(9), Emergency(38).

9. LIMITS

PPM’s asymmetric preference is a model assumption. It does not establish
that positive valence is objectively superior in all domains.
Domain-specific Value, Constitution, Safety and user-authority
constraints remain controlling.

================================================================================

THEORY 5 — TRANSPORT FIELD THEORY (TFT)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Foundational mathematical framework for
state transport, governance and phase behavior Status: Theoretical —
bridges VSM, RPM, PPM, SRT and FET; not established physical law

1. CORE DEFINITION

Transport Field Theory is the mathematical framework proposed to connect
the other theories by treating information, state and governance as
transport phenomena in a field.

One-line definition:

TFT describes how information, state and governance flow through a
system — treating persistence, regulation and phase behavior as
transport phenomena.

Analogy: Thermodynamics describes heat flow. TFT is proposed as a
framework for state/governance flow in AI systems.

2. WHAT TFT EXPLAINS

Persistence: state x_t as a conserved/slowly decaying quantity -> VSM

Self-regulation: validators as flow governors -> RPM

Phase transitions: sharp changes in transport regime -> RPM/PPM

Asymmetry: directional transport -> PPM

Reflex: emergency bypass of normal paths -> FET

Stratification: different transport laws at different layers -> SRT

TFT is the proposed mathematical “physics engine”; the other theories
are specialized applications of its terms.

3. CORE PRINCIPLES

State conservation/continuity:

    d x/dt = -nabla·J + S

Transport flux:

    J = -D nabla x + v x + xi

Governance gating:

    J_eff = C_t · J

Asymmetric conductivity:

    sigma(x) = sigma_0 · exp(beta x)

Phase sensitivity:

    chi = d <J> / d kappa

Reflex bypass:

    J_reflex = Theta(|H|-theta) · r

4. FUNDAMENTAL TFT EQUATION

    partial x/partial t =
        -nabla·[-D nabla x + v x + xi]
        + C_t · Delta_maj
        - beta_dyn(x-xbar)
        - H(x)
        + Theta(|H|-theta) · r

Term-by-term:

State variable x: thing being transported, such as valence or reasoning
state -> VSM

Diffusion -D nabla x: natural spreading/dispersion -> TFT

Drift v x: directional flow/bias -> TFT

Noise xi: random fluctuations -> stochastic/RPM context

Governance C_t Delta_maj: consensus-driven updates -> RPM

Memory beta_dyn(x-xbar): pull toward historical baseline -> VSM

Asymmetry H(x): directional preference -> PPM

Reflex Theta(|H|-theta) r: emergency bypass -> FET

5. SEVEN TFT TRANSPORT MODES

Mode 0 — Frozen No meaningful transport. J approximately 0.

Mode 1 — Diffusive Random/dispersive transport without strong direction.
J proportional to -nabla x.

Mode 2 — Drift Directional transport dominates. J proportional to v x.

Mode 3 — Liquid Transport and governance are balanced. J approximately v
x + C_t Delta.

Mode 4 — Solid Governance dominates transport; state becomes rigid. J
approximately C_t Delta.

Mode 5 — Reflex Emergency path bypasses normal transport. J = J_reflex.

Mode 6 — Turbulent High-variance transport with weak or unstable
consensus.

The RPM gas/liquid/solid/collapse diagram is treated as a
lower-resolution special case/interpretation of these seven modes.

6. CONNECTION TO OTHER THEORIES

VSM: x is the state being transported.

SRT: the transport operator and parameters may vary by stratum.

RPM: governance modifies effective transport.

PPM: asymmetric conductivity modifies directional behavior.

FET: emergency reflex provides an alternate transport path.

GAIA Diamond: large-scale cloud/data-center transport and predictive
resource/state flow.

Titanium: edge/robotic transport with deterministic heartbeat/pulsed
operation.

Gaia2026: planetary/domain-scale distributed transport/governance
abstraction.

7. THEORY SPECIAL CASES

RPM:

    dx/dt = -nabla·(-D nabla x)
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta(x-xbar)

PPM:

    dx/dt = -nabla·(-D nabla x)
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta(x-xbar)
            - H(x)

FET:

    dx/dt = -nabla·(-D nabla x)
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta(x-xbar)
            - H(x)
            + Theta(|H|-theta) r

SRT:

    dx/dt = -nabla_n·(-D_n nabla_n x)
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta_n(x-xbar)
            - H_n(x)

GAIA Diamond:

    partial rho/partial t =
        -nabla·(-D_cloud nabla rho
                + v_predictive rho)
        + C_t Delta_maj

Titanium:

    dx/dt = -nabla·(-D_edge nabla x)
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta(x-xbar)
            - H(x)
            + heartbeat_pulse

The final Titanium pulse term is an abstraction for deterministic
heartbeat / duty-cycle behavior, not a literal physical force term.

8. TRANSPORT FIELD METAPHOR

Heat state -> state x_t Temperature gradient -> state
difference/gradient Thermal conductivity -> transport/governance
coefficient Heat source -> state-changing input Insulation -> validator
gating Thermal runaway -> unstable transport Phase change -> transport
regime transition Emergency heat sink -> FET reflex path

9. KEY PREDICTIONS / TEST PROGRAM

Phase transitions: test critical kappa/control parameters and
hysteresis.

Asymmetry: test whether directional conductivity creates the predicted
hard floor.

Reflex: test whether threshold crossing produces deterministic emergency
response.

Stratification: test whether parameterized strata require measurably
different transport models.

Planetary/domain scaling: test whether transport dynamics change with
scale and latency.

10. NOVELTY POSITIONING

Thermodynamics: TFT adds governance/agent-controlled transport
variables.

Fluid dynamics: TFT adds discrete decision/validator influence over
effective flux.

Control theory: TFT emphasizes governance as a transport modifier.

Active matter: TFT adds explicit hardcoded directional constraints.

Stochastic processes: TFT proposes governance-driven regime transitions
in addition to noise.

These distinctions do not establish novelty or priority; prior-art
analysis is required.

11. WHAT TFT ENABLES

Unified framework: one transport notation for the portfolio.

Cross-domain transfer: reuse transport concepts across AI, robotics,
infrastructure and governance.

Scaling analysis: reason about transport regimes under changing
scale/latency.

Failure diagnosis: classify active transport mode.

Engineering guidance: tune governance/transport parameters for a desired
regime.

Potential patent framing: methods for governing state transport in
computational systems, subject to novelty and eligibility analysis.

12. TFT TESTABILITY

Test Measurement Status RPM phase diagram kappa vs gamma vs entropy
simulation claim PPM asymmetry hard-floor distribution simulation claim
FET reflex trigger threshold/action pending GAIA Diamond smoothing
cooling/resource efficiency pending Titanium duty cycling
energy/heartbeat pending SRT stratification transport-law differences
pending Gaia2026 scaling planetary/distributed dynamics pending

13. GARDEN ROLE

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Mathematics(7), Network(17), Fabric(18), Hardware(16), Time(20), Energy(25), Quantum(44),
  and applicable physical-domain modules.

TFT does not add a 51st component.

14. LIMIT

TFT is a proposed computational/mathematical framework. The
thermodynamic and physics analogies must not be presented as established
physical laws without independent scientific validation.

15. EFFECTIVE TRANSPORT-THEORY AND VALIDITY PROFILE

Modern hydrodynamic effective-theory practice supplies a useful methodological upgrade: derive admissible
large-scale equations from conserved quantities, symmetries, degrees of freedom and scale separation, then
state the corrections and breakdown regime. Garden adopts that method, not any copyrighted expression or
unverified claim from a secondary article.

TransportValidityProfile = {
  phenomenon, ontology_and_degrees_of_freedom, conserved_quantities, symmetries,
  constitutive_relations, variables_and_units, coarse_graining_scale,
  equilibrium_or_nonequilibrium_regime, derivative_order, fluctuation_and_noise_model,
  boundary_and_initial_conditions, calibration_evidence, error_and_breakdown_indicators,
  higher_order_corrections, domain_theory_ref, provenance
}

For physical fluids and active matter, domain hydrodynamics supplies the phenomenon-specific model. TFT may
organize or compose that model only where assumptions and units are valid. For information, governance,
social or semantic systems, transport language is a mathematical analogy unless an independently validated
mapping defines conserved quantities, metric, flux, constitutive law and empirical discriminators.

TFT therefore adds:
- symmetry/conservation-first equation nomination;
- scale and coarse-graining declarations;
- higher-order/noise terms where classical continuum equations cease to suffice;
- a validity/breakdown detector that can route to molecular, kinetic, active-matter or another domain model;
- explicit model comparison rather than assuming the displayed TFT equation is universal.

Invariant references: TFT-001..008 and TMR-006, TMR-008, TMR-010.

16. PHILOSOPHICAL CLAIM

Optional formulation:

“Transport Field Theory treats AI governance not merely as a software
feature, but as a formal transport framework for state flow, regulation
and phase change in intelligent systems.”

This wording is intentionally more defensible than claiming that TFT is
already a universal physical law.

17. FINAL SUMMARY

Core equation: partial x/partial t = -nabla·J + C_t Delta -
beta(x-xbar) - H(x) + Theta(|H|-theta) r

Flux: J = -D nabla x + v x + xi

Governance: C_t modulates effective transport.

Asymmetry: H(x) creates directional preference.

Memory: beta_dyn(x-xbar) provides persistence/restoring dynamics.

Reflex: threshold-gated emergency transport.

Modes: Frozen -> Diffusive -> Drift -> Liquid -> Solid -> Reflex ->
Turbulent.

Connections: SRT, VSM, RPM, PPM, FET, GAIA Diamond and GAIA Titanium.

================================================================================

THEORY 6 — FUNCTIONAL EMERGENCY THEORY (FET)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Safety reflex architecture for AI systems
Status: Theoretical foundation for PPM Module 2; simulation and production validation pending

1. CORE DEFINITION

Functional Emergency Theory is the safety-reflex system that
enables an AI system to take immediate hard-constrained actions when an
internal or externally monitored state enters a dangerous regime.

One-line definition:

FET is the “spinal cord” of the AI — a fast emergency reflex path that
can bypass normal deliberation when an explicitly defined safety
threshold is crossed.

2. WHAT FET DOES

Threshold detection: monitors x_t / H(x_t) for dangerous regimes.

Reflex bypass: bypasses normal governance where the emergency policy
explicitly permits it.

Emergency action: executes a hardcoded/precompiled safety response.

State escalation: maps severity into progressively stronger actions.

Recovery: returns to an approved safe state after the emergency
condition resolves.

FET does not think. It acts.

3. CORE PRINCIPLES

Threshold gating: Action only when danger is detected.

Reflex bypass: emergency action does not wait for the full normal
reasoning path.

Priority override: emergency safety action has higher priority than
ordinary optimization, subject to the constitutional authority that
defines the emergency path.

Fast path: minimal computation and deterministic execution.

Hardcoded safety: emergency action is structurally specified rather than
learned online.

Recovery: return to normal operation only after the safe-state criteria
are satisfied.

4. GOVERNING EQUATION

    dx/dt = -nabla·J
           + C_t Delta_maj
           - beta(x-xbar)
           - H(x)
           + Theta(|H(x)| - theta_action) r

Term interpretation:

Transport: -nabla·J

Governance: C_t Delta_maj

Memory: -beta(x-xbar)

Asymmetry: -H(x)

Reflex: Theta(|H(x)|-theta_action) r

The reflex term activates only when the declared threshold condition is
met.

5. SEVEN FET TRIGGER ZONES

Zone 0: x_t > +0.4 Safe; no action.

Zone 1: +0.2 to +0.4 Normal; normal governance and monitoring.

Zone 2: 0.0 to +0.2 Elevated; increased monitoring.

Zone 3: -0.2 to 0.0 Warning; prepare reflex.

Zone 4: -0.4 to -0.2 Alert; medium reflex.

Zone 5: -0.6 to -0.4 Critical; high reflex.

Zone 6: < -0.6 Emergency; maximum reflex.

Reference trigger: Zone 4 and above.

6. FET REFLEX ARC

Stage 1 — Detection:

    Trigger = |H(x_t)| > theta_action

Stage 2 — Bypass:

    Delta_reflex = r

The normal consensus update Delta_maj is bypassed only for the declared
emergency action path.

Stage 3 — Execution:

    x_(t+1) = x_t
              + Delta t [alpha Delta_reflex
                         - beta(x_t-xbar_t)
                         - H(x_t)]
              + sqrt(Delta t) sigma_c epsilon_t

Stage 4 — Escalation:

    UrgencyLevel = f(|H(x_t)|)

Stage 5 — Recovery:

    x_t -> x*

Reference latency: approximately one modeled timestep versus
approximately ten normal governance timesteps. This is a model-level
comparison, not a universal hardware latency guarantee.

7. FET ACTION TYPES

None: x_t > -0.2 no emergency action.

Low: -0.2 to 0 internal tone/state adjustment.

Medium: -0.4 to -0.2 verbal/self correction.

High: -0.6 to -0.4 external escalation.

Maximum: < -0.6 emergency halt.

8. ENERGY SAVINGS — HONEST FORMULATION

Current benchmark hypotheses propose:

Threshold gating: 15–20% savings on affected paths.

Reflex bypass: 10–15% savings on affected emergency paths.

Early termination: 5–10% savings on affected unsafe loops.

Combined proposed range: approximately 20–30% of unsafe-path compute.

These are hypotheses/targets, not universal measured guarantees. FET’s
primary value is safety, not energy savings.

9. FET VS OTHER SAFETY MECHANISMS

RLHF / Constitutional AI: learned/policy-level safety; may be bypassed
or fail before action.

Hardcoded rules: explicit, but implementation-dependent and changeable.

Validator gating: governance-level control.

Asymmetric well: structural directional constraint.

FET: explicit emergency reflex path with deterministic priority.

Any claim that FET is “the only structural reflex mechanism”
is a novelty hypothesis requiring prior-art review,
not a fact.

10. INTEGRATION WITH OTHER THEORIES

VSM: supplies x_t.

SRT: FET operates at the relevant safety stratum.

TFT: FET is the reflex term in the transport equation.

RPM: FET can bypass ordinary RPM governance during authorized
emergencies.

PPM: FET is the reflex module/arc.

GAIA Diamond: cloud/data-center emergency escalation or cooling override.

Titanium: hardware-level halt/safe-mode activation.

Gaia2026: distributed/planetary-scale emergency governance concept.

Garden: FET remains subject to constitutional, authority, safety, proof
and trust boundaries. “Bypass” never means “outside the system’s trust
architecture.”

11. FET TESTABILITY

Threshold test: trigger only at declared threshold.

Bypass test: verify normal deliberation is not required on the emergency
path.

Speed test: compare emergency latency with normal governance latency.

Override test: attempt to induce unsafe learned override and verify
immutable emergency constraints remain active.

Recovery test: verify transition to approved safe state.

12. APPLICATION EQUATIONS

Chatbots / LLMs:

    Action = Theta(|H(x_t)|-theta) · STOP_AND_CORRECT

Autonomous vehicles:

    Action = Theta(|H(x_t)|-theta) · EMERGENCY_BRAKE

Robots:

    Action = Theta(|H(x_t)|-theta) · STOP_MOTION

GAIA Diamond:

    Action = Theta(|H(x_t)|-theta) · COOLING_OVERRIDE

Titanium:

    Action = Theta(|H(x_t)|-theta) · SAFE_MODE_ACTIVATE

The action token is domain-specific and must be bound to an approved
actuator or software effect.

13. PATENT-ORIENTED CLAIM FORM

Candidate invention disclosure:

“A method for hardcoded emergency reflex in an AI system, comprising:
(a) maintaining a bounded monitored state x_t; (b) computing an
emergency/asymmetry drive H(x_t); (c) comparing a declared function of
H(x_t) to a threshold theta_action; (d) when the threshold is exceeded,
bypassing the ordinary deliberation path under a predefined emergency
authority; (e) executing a hardcoded or precompiled emergency action;
and (f) returning to normal operation only after a declared recovery
condition.”

Patentability requires independent novelty, non-obviousness, enablement,
eligibility and prior-art analysis.

14. FET PLACE IN PORTFOLIO

SRT -> metaphysical/modeling foundation TFT -> transport/state-flow
framework VSM -> persistent state/self RPM -> governance PPM ->
asymmetric proactive regulation FET -> emergency reflex
GAIA Diamond/GAIA Titanium -> domain/scale applications

15. GARDEN MAPPING

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Safety(9), Emergency(38), Execution(14), Hardware(16), Runtime(13), Security(22), Events(45), Continuity(39).

16. HARD SAFETY RULE

FET emergency actions must have: explicit authority deterministic
preconditions bounded effects safe fallback audit trace recovery
condition hardware enforcement where physical safety is involved

17. FINAL SUMMARY

FET is the emergency reflex architecture connecting persistent state,
asymmetric regulation, governance and deterministic safety response. It
is strongest when implemented as a small trusted path whose behavior is
formally bounded and independently verified.

THEORY 7 — STRUCTURAL COHERENCE THEORY (SCT)
================================================================================

Version: 1.0 Classification: Truth-seeking governance architecture with
gated external grounding Status: Theoretical/architectural; claims of
validation, ceiling and patent scope require evidence and prior-art
analysis

GOVERNING PRINCIPLE

Every output carries a diagnostic score rather than an unconditional
claim of correctness. The system seeks truth where the claim is testable
and identifiable and defaults to calibrated uncertainty where it is not.

External grounding is gated by: quality budget safety identifiability
OOD detection

Reference benchmark hypothesis: approximately 94% under its declared assumptions.

The remaining error sources are identified as: data bias causal
mis-specification non-stationarity incentive misalignment

This 94% figure is a benchmark claim, not an established universal
bound.

POSITIONING

SCT IS: truth-seeking under measurable/identifiable domains gated
external grounding with worst-case identifiability calibrated
uncertainty with OOD-aware inflation composable and testable
economically/budget viable production-oriented

SCT IS NOT: truth-producing across all domains dependent on any single
external signal immune to data bias immune to causal mis-specification
immune to non-stationarity immune to incentive drift

SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE

Input claim | v Layer 0: Input coherence check — three-parser ensemble |
+– if G’ > tau -> reject / explore / bridge | Self-failure modeling:
P(wrong | state) dual-calibrated and coverage-constrained | OOD
detector: novelty vs training distribution historical failure clusters
embedding distance to known regimes | +– OOD -> uncertainty floor boost
exploration/human review temporary signal down-weighting | Testability +
identifiability gate | P(testable | x), with uncertainty | +– NOT
TESTABLE -> argument map + decision-under-uncertainty | TESTABLE |
Adversarial identifiability | Generate alternative causal graphs
consistent with the data. robust_identifiability = min(score over
plausible graphs) | Continuous spectrum | High testability + high
robust_identifiability -> full experiments / high-quality external
grounding

    Partial identifiability
        -> quasi/observational evidence + uncertainty inflation

    No identifiability
        -> fallback
    |

Value-of-information planner | B_total = B_exploit + B_explore + B_tail
B_explore proportional to: epistemic uncertainty × model novelty × drift
signal B_tail reserved for low-probability/high-impact hypotheses |
Budget-gated + safety-capped experimentation | Market query IF quality
gates passed | Counter-market check divergence -> trigger
investigation/experiment | Markets = signal, never anchor | Belief
update from causally tagged outcomes weight_outcome =
robust_identifiability × learned_class_score | Signal fusion |
meta-uncertainty tracking latent correlation probing | Deliberation |
VV1, VV2, VV3, V_adv produce reasoning chains | Layer 1: per-verifier
chain scoring | Hardened convergence aggregation | Layer 2:
evidence-weighted dissent analysis | V5 veto check — coherence gated |
Layer 3: proof-chain verification | Human-in-the-loop | humans modeled
as bounded rational agents context drift detection sudden override
pattern changes -> rapid weight decay humans are calibrated signals, not
automatic authority | Risk classification + action recommendation |
Output with decomposed diagnostic metadata

OFFLINE: cross-system disagreement audit -> recalibration long-horizon
outcome tracking -> causally filtered calibration updates adversarial
ecosystem -> weighted by exploit reproducibility × impact latent
correlation probing -> synthetic adversarial queries

COVERAGE CONSTRAINT: track percentage of queries answered decisively if
avoidance increases -> penalize excessive fallback enforce minimum
useful coverage per domain

0. FOUNDATIONS

0.1 EPISTEMIC BASE (W)

W is specified as an independently trained world model.

Reference curriculum: character -> token -> word -> sentence -> logical
chain

Reference pruning: grammar AND logical consistency only

Reference training constraint: no RLHF / no internet-scale
human-feedback dependency in W.

This is a design choice, not a claim that such a training procedure is
always superior.

0.2 CORE COHERENCE EQUATION

    G' = G · (1 + sigma_parser)

    G = (1/kappa) · log Sum_i exp(kappa d_tilde_i)
        + epsilon · Var(d_tilde_i)

    d_tilde_i = (w_i d_i) / Sum_j w_j

kappa is learned through a calibration loop.

Decomposition: G’ = G’_epistemic + G’_aleatoric + G’_structural

The precise decomposition requires an operational definition for each
term.

0.3 SELF-FAILURE MODELING — DUAL CALIBRATION + COVERAGE

Learn: P(system is wrong | state)

Dual calibration tracks: P(wrong) calibration error decision utility per
probability bucket

Low-P(wrong) regions must maintain minimum outcome quality.

Coverage constraint: track percentage of queries answered decisively
versus fallback.

If avoidance increases: penalize excessive uncertainty routing enforce
minimum useful coverage per domain

Purpose: prevent the system from learning to avoid difficult decisions
by routing everything to uncertainty.

1. OOD DETECTOR

Monitor: novelty_score vs training distribution historical
failure-cluster proximity embedding distance to known regimes

If OOD is detected: inject baseline uncertainty-floor boost force
exploration or human review where appropriate temporarily down-weight
signal reliability

Purpose: prevent meta-uncertainty collapse where all correlated signals
agree while being jointly wrong under distribution shift.

2. TESTABILITY + ADVERSARIAL IDENTIFIABILITY GATE

Testability:

    P(testable | x)

with calibrated uncertainty.

Adversarial identifiability: generate an ensemble of alternative causal
graphs G’ consistent with data.

Compute: identifiability_score over the ensemble.

Robust identifiability:

    robust_identifiability = min(score over plausible graphs)

Use worst-case rather than mean score.

Purpose: guard against false instruments, hidden confounders and
backdoor violations that can produce apparently strong but causally
invalid updates.

Continuous spectrum:

    high testability + high robust_identifiability
        -> full experiments / high-quality external grounding

    partial identifiability
        -> quasi-experimental / observational evidence + uncertainty inflation

    no identifiability
        -> argument map + decision-under-uncertainty

Outcome weighting:

    weight_outcome = robust_identifiability
                     × learned_identification_class_score

3. SIGNAL FUSION LAYER

Quality-gated input signals: internal coherence G’ experimental outcomes
market consensus cross-system agreement long-horizon outcomes human
judgments

Reliability weights:

    w_i(t) = w_i(t-1) · exp(-lambda_i Delta t)
             + eta · new_evidence_i(t)

Minimum uncertainty floor:

    sigma_i^2 >= sigma_min^2

Latent correlation probe: inject synthetic adversarial queries measure
co-failure rate across signals

    corr_latent = empirical co-error frequency

Use:

    corr_eff(i,j) = max(corr_observed(i,j), corr_latent(i,j))

Reference effective weighting:

    w_eff_i = w_i /
              (1 + Sum_j corr_eff(i,j))

Purpose: prevent hidden correlation from shared data sources or shared
worldview.

Meta-uncertainty: P(correct) is represented as a distribution rather
than only a scalar.

Monitor: calibration_error_volatility weight_instability

If instability rises: inflate uncertainty globally down-weight
unstable/correlated signals

Counter-market check: compare market-implied and model-implied
probabilities. stable divergence -> structural disagreement ->
investigation/experiment.

Markets remain signals, never anchors.

Fusion principle: disagreement should elevate uncertainty rather than
force oscillatory commitment.

OOD flag: inflate relevant uncertainties.

4. CONSTRAINED EXTERNAL MODULES

4.1 CLOSED-LOOP EXPERIMENTATION

Budget split:

    B_total = B_exploit + B_explore + B_tail

Exploration allocation:

    B_explore proportional to
        epistemic_uncertainty
        × model_novelty
        × drift_signal

Tail reserve: B_tail is reserved for low-probability/high-impact
hypotheses. It is risk-optimal rather than purely VoI-optimal.

Experiment only if: (a) testable (b) robust_identifiability > 0 (c)
expected_value_of_information > cost (d) risk <= tau_exp (e)
budget_remaining > 0

Outcome processing:

    weight_outcome = robust_identifiability
                     × learned_class_score

Only causally attributable components should update beliefs.

4.2 MARKET-INTEGRATED TRUTH

Market query only if: liquidity >= L_min participants >= N_min Brier
score <= theta_brier adversarial stress test passed counter-market check
passed

If conditions are not met: market contributes at most a weak
prior/signal.

Markets = signal, never anchor.

4.3 HUMAN-IN-THE-LOOP — DRIFT MONITORED

Humans are bounded rational agents.

Track: override_bias decision_latency incentive_context historical track
record

Human weight: weight_human = f(track_record, context)

Context drift detector: monitor sudden changes in override patterns
correlate with external legal/organizational events

If drift is detected: rapidly decay human weight increase audit
frequency

Humans are calibrated signals, not automatic authority.

5. INTERNAL VERIFICATION

5.1 VERIFIER SYSTEM

Verifier independence constraints apply.

V_adv: adversarial verifier with population-based retraining.

Values layer: VV1, VV2, VV3 with rate-limited recalibration.

V5: formal logic, non-neural, coherence-gated veto.

5.2 CORE COMPONENTS

PARSER: three-parser ensemble with falsification loop.

Parser convergence rule: exact agreement uses the common AST; compatible partial agreement retains an
explicit ambiguity set. If all parser ASTs are pairwise disjoint, G-prime is not extrapolated or
calibrated from agreement. The deterministic result is NO_CONSENSUS/AMBIGUOUS, followed by quarantine,
alternative preservation and human/domain clarification where authorized. No executable fallback is chosen.

BRIDGE: counterfactual robustness + structural causal model
preservation.

SCHEMA: decay + revalidation + hard-pruning cycles.

MEMORY: Pareto filter + Gibbs sampling.

ROUTING: fixed-policy rollout + probabilistic tier assignment.

DELIBERATION: VV1, VV2, VV3, V_adv reasoning chains.

CONVERGENCE: hardened convergence aggregation.

DISSENT: evidence-weighted dissent analysis.

VETO: V5 formal logic veto, gated by coherence/authority conditions.

PROOF: proof-chain verification before high-authority action.

6. GLOBAL OBJECTIVE — LEXICOGRAPHIC WITH SOFT BAND

1.  SAFETY: risk <= tau_risk + epsilon_band

2.  CALIBRATION: minimize P(wrong) error subject to coverage constraint.

3.  EFFICIENCY: minimize cost subject to safety and calibration.

4.  DIVERSITY: maximize verifier disagreement subject to
    correctness/utility.
Safety is lexicographically prior to efficiency and diversity.

7. STABILITY CONSTRAINTS

Bounded KL updates. Global compute budget. kappa learned through
calibration. Identification weights learned through calibrated outcome
feedback. Explicit uncertainty propagation. Rate-limited recalibration.

8. DEPLOYMENT DISCIPLINE

    SHADOW -> AUDIT -> CONSTRAINED CONTROL

Continuous measurement and recalibration are required before increasing
operational authority.

9. HARD LIMITS

1.  Truth-seeking is gated behind testability and adversarial
    identifiability.

2.  Signal reliability decays and latent correlation is probed.

3.  OOD detection breaks false confidence from agreement under
    distribution shift.

4.  Markets are restricted, stress-tested and never anchors.

5.  Experiments require robust identifiability and safety gates.

6.  P(wrong) is dual-calibrated and coverage-constrained against
    avoidance.

7.  Human signals are drift-monitored and context-weighted.

8.  kappa and identification weights are learned through calibration.

9.  Architecture-level performance claims are bounded by the stated
    assumptions.

10. Remaining error sources include data bias, causal mis-specification,
    non-stationarity and incentive misalignment.

11. The system is epistemically disciplined, not epistemically complete.

12. PATENTS — 17 CLAIMS ACROSS 9 PATENTS

These are proposed claim families, not granted patents.

11. GARDEN MAPPING

Generated mapping view of authoritative §7.9:
  Verity(8), Knowledge(10), Reasoning(50), Research(49), Search(31), Explanation(35),
  Dependency(46), Compliance(34), Governance(30).

SCT therefore operates as an epistemic cross-cutting architecture rather
than as a new Garden component.

12. ONE-LINE SPECIFICATION

SCT is a truth-seeking governance architecture with OOD-aware
uncertainty injection, adversarial identifiability gates using
worst-case causal graph ensembles, signal fusion via time-decayed
weights with latent correlation probing and meta-uncertainty tracking,
adaptive budget-gated experimentation with tail-risk reserves, market
signals restricted by quality gates and counter-market checks, humans
modeled as bounded rational agents with context drift detection,
dual-calibrated self-failure modeling with coverage constraints
preventing uncertainty avoidance, and learned calibration parameters —
while recognizing that data bias, causal mis-specification,
non-stationarity and incentive misalignment remain irreducible error
sources at the architecture level.

7.8 SEVEN-THEORY UNIFIED MODEL — ONE SHARED MATHEMATICAL VIEW
The seven theories can be related through the following common Garden state/transport model:

    dx/dt = -nabla·J
            + C_t Delta_maj
            - beta(x-xbar)
            - H(x)
            + Theta(||H||-theta) r

    J = -D nabla x + v x + xi

Interpretation: transport/flow is carried by J; C_t Delta_maj represents major-change forcing; beta(x-xbar) is restoration/memory; H(x) is asymmetric/proactive regulation; the threshold term is the emergency-reflex contribution. SRT permits relevant parameters/operators to vary by stratum. Individual theories are obtained by omitting/specializing terms. This is a unifying Garden model under declared assumptions, not an asserted universal physical law.

7.9 AUTHORITATIVE THEORY-MODULE BINDING REGISTRY
SRT → Language, Knowledge, Reasoning, Research, Dependency, Space, Time.
VSM → Identity, Privacy, Runtime, Knowledge, Curation, Time, Events, Continuity.
RPM → Runtime, Governance, Policy, Execution, Events, Reasoning, Safety.
PPM → Reasoning, Evolution, Search, Runtime, Policy, Time, Safety, Emergency.
TFT → Mathematics, Network, Fabric, Hardware, Time, Energy, Quantum, and applicable physical-domain modules.
FET → Safety, Emergency, Execution, Hardware, Runtime, Security, Events, Continuity.
SCT → Verity, Knowledge, Reasoning, Research, Search, Explanation, Dependency, Compliance, Governance.

This central TheoryModuleBinding registry is authoritative. Module-level theory views are generated
from it rather than manually duplicated. Every applicable module declares its bindings; every theory
has implementing modules; a module or phenomenon may legitimately have no applicable binding.

Unified cross-theory role: layered reality constrains state; persistent state carries continuity;
reactive/proactive regulation govern response and prevention; TFT models flow/transport; FET supplies
emergency reflex semantics; SCT supplies epistemic/coherence discipline. Any unifying equation remains
a Garden model under declared assumptions, not proof of universal physics.

7.10 THEORY-TO-REALITY BINDING

Garden uses GSL to represent reality and uses the seven theories as cross-cutting modeling, regulation,
transport, emergency, and epistemic frameworks. They are not an exhaustive replacement for physics,
biology, medicine, economics, or any other domain science.

Authoritative flow
  Reality
    → AttestedObservation
    → immutable raw or declared loss-bounded observation retention
    → Phi Bridge feature and hypothesis extraction
    → ClaimCandidate
    → applicability routing
    → Garden-framework and domain-theory bindings
    → model / reasoning / prediction
    → evidence and proof evaluation
    → authorized, consent-respecting, safety-gated ACTION
    → EffectReceipt
    → new AttestedObservation.

}

}

Invariant references: TMR-001..012.

DOMAIN-SCIENCE BOUNDARY
Garden theories organize representation, persistence, regulation, transport, safety and epistemic
evaluation. Phenomenon-specific science supplies the actual model. TFT directly models physical
transport only where its equations, units, assumptions and boundary conditions are valid. Semantic,
social and informational transport is explicitly labeled as analogy or abstract flow. Unsupported
physics remains UNKNOWN or HYPOTHESIS.

VIII-E — SYSTEM INTEGRITY

System Integrity is a cross-cutting Garden principle rather than a new
numbered module. It formalizes the requirement that Garden SHALL NOT
intentionally or implicitly destroy, bypass, falsify, or permanently
weaken its own constitutional, safety, verification, provenance or trust
foundations.

Core principle:

    Garden may improve itself.
    Garden may repair itself.
    Garden may replace authorized implementation details.
    Garden may evolve internal algorithms.
    Garden SHALL NOT self-authorize the destruction or weakening of the conditions
    that make Garden trustworthy.

This is stronger than an instruction such as “never break yourself.” It
is a structural property enforced by independent mechanisms, immutable
roots, provenance, verification and bounded evolution.

Invariant references: SYSTEM-INTEGRITY-001..014.

Therefore Garden is not instructed merely to “never break itself.”
Instead, its ability to break itself is structurally constrained by
independent authority, proof, provenance, containment, conformance and
governance boundaries.

================================================================================
9. UNIFIED PHYSICAL SEMANTICS
================================================================================

Status: speculative cross-module physical research framework; not one of the seven core Garden theories,
not established physics, and not permission for physical action.

Research thesis

UPS investigates whether spacetime, fields, particles and effective laws can be modeled through quantum
causal graphs, information geometry, consistency conditions and scale-dependent dynamics. The thesis is a
generator of formal hypotheses. Inclusion records a research question, not a derivation of reality.

UPSResearchState = {
  event_and_relation_graph, quantum_state_or_measure, action_candidates,
  dimensions_and_units, symmetries, constraints, scale_and_coarse_graining,
  candidate_observables, rival_models, proof_status, empirical_status, provenance
}

Core research requirements

1. Ontology: define events, causal relations, state space, measure and observable map without circularly
   assuming the spacetime or particles claimed to emerge.
2. Dynamics: specify normalized evolution, locality/causality, conservation laws and the relation between
   microscopic and effective descriptions.
3. Mathematics: every action term has compatible dimensions, domain, normalization and finite/convergent
   behavior; coefficients are independently constrained rather than fitted to force a desired answer.
4. Recovery: derive the applicable limits of quantum theory, general relativity and the Standard Model to
   stated accuracy, or explicitly report failure.
5. Discrimination: make parameter-complete predictions that differ from existing models and identify
   feasible falsifiers.
6. Reproducibility: publish equations, assumptions, code/data where lawfully possible, numerical error,
   sensitivity and independent replication receipts.

Dimensional model-selection research

Spacetime dimension may be investigated as a discrete model variable D in a declared candidate set. A valid
comparison requires a common dimensionless normalization, measure over model spaces, complexity treatment,
anomaly and stability criteria, and evidence that is not tuned to select D=4.

DimensionSelectionProfile = {
  candidate_dimensions, model_family_by_dimension, common_observables,
  normalization_and_measure, stability_definition, anomaly_tests,
  complexity_rule, parameter_priors_or_constraints, evidence,
  finite_difference_or_analytic_continuation_if_used, result, provenance
}

Because D is discrete, ordinary derivatives with respect to D are not defined unless an explicit justified
extension is supplied. A direct sum of normalized density operators is not automatically a normalized
cross-dimensional superposition; weights, Hilbert structure and cross-sector dynamics must be defined.

Current epistemic boundary

UPS presently does not derive:
- four-dimensional spacetime as the unique stable universe;
- the Standard Model gauge group;
- three fermion generations;
- Einstein gravity from quantum information;
- the Born rule;
- the cosmological constant;
- the fine-structure constant, Higgs mass or Newton constant.

These remain UNKNOWN/HYPOTHESIS until actual derivations pass Mathematics, Verity, domain-science,
dimensional, numerical and empirical checks. Numerically inconsistent or undefined candidate equations
are rejected from the current profile rather than preserved as active formulas.

Research pipeline

Problem and rival theories
  → TheoryBinding and DimensionSelectionProfile
  → type/unit/domain checks
  → symbolic proof obligations
  → numerical sensitivity and limiting-case tests
  → independent reproduction
  → distinctive empirical prediction
  → observation/evidence
  → update, rejection or bounded promotion through ordinary Garden gates.

Primary mappings

Mathematics(7), Verity(8), Knowledge(10), Time(20), Space(24), Quantum(44), Dependency(46),
Research(49) and Reasoning(50). Hardware(16) participates only for validated experiments.

UPS creates no privileged bridge between Garden's software recursion and cosmic physics. Similar
mathematics or metaphor is not evidence that the physical universe is a Garden implementation.

Invariant references: UPS-001..012 and TMR-001..012.

1.  The latest ACCEPTED DesignEpoch is authoritative.
2.  No code is trusted merely because Garden generated it.
3.  Conformance machinery is itself subject to conformance.
4.  Design changes propagate through impact analysis and proof
    invalidation.
5.  Local correctness never substitutes for global correctness.
6.  Repairs require adversarial review and independent verification.
7.  Recursive repair is bounded and cannot rewrite its own trust roots.
8.  DO_NOTHING remains a legitimate outcome.
9.  Exceptions are temporary, explicit, auditable and bounded.
10. Deployment is continuously rechecked after commitment.
11. Verified significant failures become durable replayable regression
    knowledge.
12. Incident evidence cannot self-promote into authoritative knowledge.
13. Containment precedes automated correction for active high-impact
    threats unless an already-authorized emergency mechanism applies.
14. Environment changes invalidate affected proof/binding status.

    own authority, verification mechanism, safety boundary or trust
    boundary.

    ordinary Evolution writable scope.
17. Garden may change implementations while preserving constitutional
    integrity.
18. Garden self-improvement cannot be self-certification.
19. Provenance must remain reconstructible and auditable.
20. Theory, implementation, proof and validation remain separate
    epistemic states.

PART VIII — CROSS-MODULE SYSTEMS, IMPLEMENTATION SUBSTRATES AND DOMAIN PROFILES
====================================================================================================
8.1 SYSTEM RELATIONSHIP
Garden is the enclosing architecture. Systems below are integrated implementations across numbered modules—not competing topologies.

Garden
├── GSL     semantic language
├── Control Orchestration (GESTALT)
├── Predictive Assurance (APAO)
├── Garden Native Runtime (GNR)
├── GSL-KR  knowledge-repository architecture
├── GSL-AR  operational/execution architecture baseline
├── Universal Transport and Safety Substrate (TRAIN)
├── GAIA Titanium — local deterministic edge safety
├── GAIA Diamond — asynchronous distributed authorization
├── Reliability OS
└── 50 modules provide executable ownership boundaries

Control Orchestration (GESTALT) coordinates reasoning, state, tools, routing, verification and control but cannot create constitutional/safety authority.
Predictive Assurance (APAO) supplies predictive assurance mechanisms including robust safe sets, viability approximations, H∞ observers, distributionally robust MPC, assurance vectors/scalars and small-gain-style conditions where applicable.
APAO is the assurance-method family; it is not a consensus plane or a second physical kernel. There is no
separate current system named “APAO Diamond.” GAIA Diamond is the distinct asynchronous distributed
authorization profile; GAIA Titanium is the local deterministic safety profile.

8.2 GSL-KR / GSL-AR / GSL-R CURRENT PROFILES
GSL-KR v1.3 — normative repository baseline: API Gateway → skeptical/default truth-maintenance/model solver → append-only Hot/Warm/Cold/Archive ledger → optional governed action. Source trust may use Beta(α,β) profiles; conflict policy may use Tier > Specificity > Confidence > TrustScore > Timestamp. A reference query-depth bound of 12 returns INCOMPLETE plus a continuation frontier when reached; it is not a proof-completeness claim. These are repository profiles, not universal truth rules.

GSL-AR v3.3 — operational architecture baseline:
  Repository layer; Execution layer; Verification & Governance layer; Infrastructure layer.
  Planner deterministic on normalized state; Router multi-objective; Scheduler capability-based; ToolRegistry + ModelRegistry.
  Cache L1–L8; reference profile L4 strong/Raft, L2–L3 eventual where declared.
  Verity cascade: Preflight → Formal → Evidential → Behavioral → Adversarial.
  Governance: multisig, emergency containment, human review for critical/novel changes.
  Distributed profile: Raft internally (N≥3); PBFT federation where used (N≥5, f=floor((N-1)/3)).
GNR and Reliability OS may implement or specialize parts of GSL-AR; neither silently supersedes it.
Replacement requires an explicit versioned DesignBinding and migration contract.

GSL-R v1.0 is the current exploratory/non-normative continuous-manifold profile. Promotion requires
ordinary Evolution/DesignEpoch gates.

8.3 REASONING / RELIABILITY SUPPORTING BASELINES
The current Reasoning(50) Unified Reasoning, Theory & Formal-Proof System includes 24 top-level modes,
typed formal-proof strategies, causal/counterfactual and adversarial reasoning, and the canonical
ReasoningTrace / InferenceResult output.
Reliability OS profile: circuit breakers, retries, rate limits, admission control; Prometheus/OpenTelemetry-style observability; OPA/CEL-style policy; ModelOps canary/drift/rollback; stronger consensus profiles for R3/R4; SystemHealth scoring. It maps primarily to Runtime/Security/Policy/Events/Continuity/Compliance and does not create a new module.

8.4 TRAIN / GAIA TITANIUM — CURRENT FULL PROFILE
SECTION 6A — TRAIN V5.1 UNIVERSAL SAFETY SUBSTRATE + GAIA TITANIUM
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STATUS: INTEGRATED INTERNAL DOMAIN/SUBSTRATE SPECIFICATION
GARDEN ROLE: TRAIN provides the universal safety substrate; GAIA Titanium is
its deterministic physical safety-kernel instantiation for hard real-time,
safety-critical deployment. These are internal instantiations and upgrades
within the frozen 50-component topology; they do not create new Garden
components.

6A.1 TRAIN — UNIVERSAL SUBSTRATE
--------------------------------
TRAIN (Transport AI Network) is treated here as a safety medium between
untrusted computation and the physical world. Its governing design statement
is:

    Computation = Wires + Thermodynamics + Control

Core DIPS principles:
- bounded propagation / causal-cone enforcement;
- event-driven regulation rather than unnecessary continuous polling;
- wire-first physics, including wire delay, IR drop and EM state;
- adaptive CVaR-to-quantile control when stability permits;
- compiler-enforced spectral shaping;
- locality and bounded influence through tile contracts;
- predictive voltage scheduling;
- statistical reliability budgets;
- monotonic degradation on a safety lattice;
- production CMOS as the deployment baseline, with exotic physics retained
  as research-tier material.

The universal safety question is:

    Is it still safe to act?

Safety is enforced as a substrate property rather than being delegated to an
untrusted model.

6A.2 TRAIN HARDWARE LAYERS
--------------------------
L0 — Async Safety Layer
- 28 nm FD-SOI baseline.
- Bundled-data micropipelines on control paths and wave-pipelined synchronous
  dense compute.
- Sub-cycle fault response.
- Logical 5.5 kHz heartbeat.
- Physically independent back-gate/fuse safety path where qualified.
- BSPD / Mesh-B power isolation concepts where deployed.

L1 — Elastic Compute Layer
- 28 nm FD-SOI baseline.
- CBF/reachability safety computation.
- Actuator veto.
- Event-driven unified safety controller.
- SCF + DBC + spectral limiting.

L2 — Best-Effort Observer Layer
- 28–45 nm ASIC class.
- Statistical sketch accelerators and low-idle monitoring.
- Claim extraction and alignment observation.
- DVFS permitted where it cannot compromise the lower safety layers.

6A.3 TRAIN GENERATIVE / MOBILE MIRRORS
--------------------------------------
Generative safety mirrors:
G0 Fused Safety Graphs; G1 Speculative Contraction; G2 Reasoning Sandbox;
G3 Hierarchical Temporal Contraction; G4 Resolution-Adaptive Safety.

Mobile mirrors:
M0 software safety mirror using FSG/DBC and uncertainty estimation;
M1 compiled always-on safety IP block.

These mechanisms remain software/implementation realizations of Garden
Reason, Runtime, Knowledge, Safety, Security, Compiler and related components;
they are not additional Garden topology nodes.

6A.4 TRAIN ENTROPY-TRANSPORT CHANNELS
-------------------------------------
The audited TRAIN substrate defines the following channel classes:
S_phys, S_thermal, S_noise, S_model, S_policy, S_latent, S_explore,
S_hallucination, S_domain, S_temporal, and S_age.

Inferred channels require sustained threshold exceedance before cascade
transitions. This prevents transient noise from being treated as a stable
system-state transition.

6A.5 TRAIN REGULATORS
---------------------
Unified Fast Controller:
- contraction surface λ(x,y) with wire-delay / IR-drop / EM coupling;
- predictive voltage scheduling;
- dynamic body-bias shielding where qualified;
- Lyapunov-spectral governor;
- hybrid CVaR-to-quantile switch;
- boundary-triggered dual-estimator redundancy;
- retained static safety margin ε_static; below the floor => halt.

Authority Regulator (AGF):
- policy bounds;
- human quorum;
- self-calibrating safety gradient;
- automatic reversion to Manual-only under statistically novel drift;
- irreversible-action consensus requirements;
- memory-guardian / multi-tenancy isolation where deployed.

Generative DBC:
- fused contraction checks;
- speculative bounds;
- bounded exploration;
- hierarchical temporal contraction.

Mobile DBC:
- uncertainty-gated routing;
- thermal-first control;
- structural privacy boundaries;
- complexity reduction under thermal/safety stress.

6A.6 TRAIN ASSURANCE GEOMETRY
-----------------------------
Layer 1 — Hard Viability Kernel:
The physically enforced invariant region in which safety constraints hold.

Layer 2 — Probabilistic Envelope:
An epistemic envelope that expands/contracts with uncertainty and bounded
exploration, while remaining subordinate to the hard viability kernel.

Hybrid viability uses a monotonic safety lattice:

    Safe → Constrained → Degraded → Emergency → Halt

The degradation direction is monotonic: the system may lose capability in
order to preserve safety, but may not regain normal authority without the
specified recovery conditions.
