6A.8 TRAIN DEGRADATION LATTICE
------------------------------
Hardware degradation follows a bounded safety ordering, with terminal halt
for unsheddable deception or equivalent catastrophic conditions.
Generative degradation tightens exploration/contraction and then constrains
or halts output.
Mobile degradation prioritizes thermal protection, Φ-gating, complexity
reduction and safe-mode operation.
Aging state S_age may prioritize remaining high-stability cycles for
Sovereign/high-criticality tasks.

6A.9 TRAIN MEMORY / RTL / COMPILER SUBSTRATE
---------------------------------------------
Memory mechanisms include probabilistic ECC, entropy-aware cache eviction,
near-memory safety computation and strict safety-region coherence.

Verification-by-construction is expressed through:
- invariant-carrying modules (ICM);
- constraint-first RTL generation;
- Safety ISA operations such as CHECK_CONTRACTION, BOUND_ACTION and
  REPORT_ENTROPY;
- DIPS-HL compiler generation of safety constraints;
- generative physical verification where qualified;
- per-tile certification hashes;
- compatibility with conventional EDA flows;
- compiler wire-energy shaping.

These are mapped to the Garden Compiler, Runtime, Hardware, Safety,
Knowledge, Research and Verity responsibilities as applicable.

6A.10 GAIA TITANIUM — DETERMINISTIC SAFETY KERNEL
--------------------------------------------------
GAIA Titanium is the hard-real-time physical kernel of the TRAIN substrate.
It is a buildable subset/instantiation for ASIL-D / SIL-3-class deployment
requirements. It is not a new Garden component.

System topology:
- heterogeneous multi-core SoC;
- bare-metal or certified Type-1 hypervisor execution;
- hard-real-time execution;
- hardware-locked 5.5 kHz heartbeat;
- 181.8 μs nominal execution window per cycle.

Layer 0 — Hard Real-Time Timing & Power Engine
- hardware timer interrupt every 181.8 μs;
- duty-cycling / DVFS when no dynamic state change is required;
- prioritized interrupt service;
- deterministic safety-path latency;
- safety-critical timing is not delegated to best-effort AI software.

Layer 1 — Predictive-Barrier Control (PB-CBF)
- physical safety boundaries represented as CBF manifolds;
- mandatory 100 ms look-ahead projection;
- forward-invariance condition:

    ḣ(x,u) + α(h(x)) ≥ 0

  where h(x) is the safety-manifold function and α is a class-K function;
- if the projected state violates the admissible safety manifold, the
  command path is intercepted and a deterministic braking/safe-halt
  trajectory is selected.

The 100 ms look-ahead is an implementation parameter of this Titanium
instantiation, not a universal Garden invariant.

Layer 2 — Pre-Authorized Capability Intake
- wide-area Byzantine consensus never runs inside the 181.8 microsecond Titanium tick;
- GAIA Diamond operates asynchronously and ahead of execution using heterogeneous probabilistic and
  deterministic symbolic validators under its declared fault model;
- GAIA Diamond emits a signed, expiring, replay-protected CapabilityCommandToken bound to device, command
  envelope, DesignEpoch, KnowledgeSnapshot, policy root, state assumptions and resource limits;
- Titanium verifies the token locally using bounded cryptographic hardware/cache and independently
  applies current physical, symbolic and K0P checks;
- a valid GAIA Diamond token grants bounded eligibility only. It never compels actuation and never overrides
  Titanium, Constitution, local authority, consent or K0P.

6A.11 TITANIUM SINGLE-TICK EXECUTION PIPELINE
---------------------------------------------
1. Atomically ingest a complete, timestamped local sensor frame and a pre-authorized GAIA Diamond token.
2. Verify token signature, epoch, scope, expiry, nonce, revocation status and local device binding.
3. Measure observation age and project the command through the PB-CBF horizon from expected actuation
   time, accounting for sensing, computation and actuator delay.
4. Apply local structural, physical, Justice, Constitution and K0P checks.
5. Dispatch only if every local gate passes; otherwise execute a precompiled constrained fallback,
   physical disconnect or safe-halt as the domain requires.
6. Journal the decision asynchronously without delaying the physical veto path.
7. Enter the qualified low-power state until the next heartbeat where permitted.

6A.12 TITANIUM → TRAIN CORRESPONDENCE
-------------------------------------
Titanium mechanism                    TRAIN substrate correspondence
5.5 kHz heartbeat                     L0 async safety / logical heartbeat
PB-CBF + 100 ms look-ahead            robust CBF / reachability / DR-MPC
Pre-authorized GAIA Diamond token          asynchronous BFT/cross-attestation;
                                     local eligibility, never local safety authority
Symbolic veto                         Representation Bridge + theorem-prover
                                     boundary
Deterministic braking / safe halt     L0 actuator veto / independent fallback
DVFS micro-sleep                      Φ-gating / entropy-gated clocking
Hard RTOS / bare-metal                L1 elastic compute safety execution

6A.13 TITANIUM DEPLOYMENT SCOPE
--------------------------------
Titanium is appropriate as a deterministic physical kernel pattern for
actuator-connected systems such as autonomous vehicles, industrial robotics,
industrial control and aerospace-class systems, subject to domain-specific
certification and empirical validation.

It intentionally does not replace the wider TRAIN layers for:
- long-timescale value/alignment stability;
- multi-agent fleet safety;
- generative-media safety;
- universal chip-development doctrine;
- full entropy-transport monitoring.

6A.14 GARDEN 50-COMPONENT MAPPING
---------------------------------
Primary mappings are:
- Safety(9): CBF, viability, degradation and safety envelopes.
- Knowledge(10): verified facts, evidence, model/environment state.
- Runtime(13): lifecycle, timing, queues and bounded execution.
- Execution(14): admissible action dispatch and safe-halt execution.
- Fabric(18): inter-module / inter-node transport and consensus substrate.
- Time(20): deterministic heartbeat, temporal state and bounded look-ahead.
- Mobility(23): autonomous movement and actuation domains.
- Security(22): attestation, Byzantine fault handling and trust boundaries.
- Hardware(16): physical veto, timing substrate and hardware enforcement.
- Reasoning(50): model intent, planning and symbolic verification boundary.
- Verity(8): proof/evidence/verification of admissibility.
- Compilation(42): constraint-first compilation, Safety ISA and generated safety
  artifacts.
- Governance(30): authority boundaries, human quorum and irreversible-action
  governance.
- Evolution(33): controlled upgrades, proof gates and rollback.
- Research(49): unresolved theorem / hardware-qualification frontiers.

These are functional responsibility mappings, not additions to the topology.
The 50-component seal remains unchanged.

6A.15 GARDEN SAFETY CLAIM BOUNDARY
----------------------------------
The integrated TRAIN/Titanium material is specification-level architecture.
Statements about ASIL-D/SIL-3 certification, collision reduction, energy
savings, latency, or production validation remain claims requiring external
qualification and GSSCC evidence unless separately discharged.

The Garden stage ladder therefore remains:

    MAPPED ≠ PROVEN ≠ IMPLEMENTED ≠ VALIDATED

Global proof obligations P1–P24 and profile obligations TRAIN-P21…TRAIN-P24 remain pending unless independently
machine-checked and accepted under the POG discharge criteria. Simulation or
architecture description does not by itself constitute production validation.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SECTION 6B — GAIA DIAMOND / GESTALT / APAO INTEGRATION RELATIONSHIP
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
GAIA Diamond, GESTALT and APAO remain internal Garden integrations already
present in the canonical topology. Titanium is the deterministic physical
safety-kernel specialization beneath/alongside these higher-level control and
assurance mechanisms.

The integration hierarchy is:

    Garden
      ├── Semantic substrate: GSL
      ├── Cognitive/control plane: GESTALT
      ├── Predictive safety / assurance: APAO
      ├── Physical deterministic safety kernel: GAIA Titanium and GAIA Diamond
      └── Universal safety substrate: TRAIN

Where mechanisms overlap, the more specific mechanism is treated as an
instantiation or implementation profile of the broader Garden invariant,
not as a second competing architecture.

Existing mappings for PB-CBF, ISS-CBF, robust safe sets, viability inner
approximation, H∞ observers, DR-MPC, assurance vectors/scalars, small-gain
conditions and TRAIN-P21…TRAIN-P24 remain in force and are cross-referenced here rather
than duplicated as independent theories.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

8.5 GAIA DIAMOND / TITANIUM — CONSOLIDATED ASYNCHRONOUS CONTRACT
B.2 — ASYNCHRONOUS DISTRIBUTED AUTHORIZATION + LOCAL DETERMINISTIC SAFETY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Status: internal implementation/deployment profile; NOT a new Garden module. GAIA Diamond is the
asynchronous distributed assurance/authorization plane. GAIA Titanium is the isolated hard-real-time
edge safety kernel and sole actuator-command authority. Network consensus is physically excluded from
the Titanium tick.

Layer 0 heartbeat: nominal 5.5 kHz / 181.8 μs cycle; reference deadline 150 μs; jitter <5 μs; hardware timer; deadline miss→watchdog safe-halt; DVFS micro-sleep between ticks when static. Values are deployment references, not universal invariants.
Layer 1 predictive-barrier control: CBF invariant hdot(x,u)+alpha(h(x))>=0; reference 100 ms look-ahead each cycle; predicted manifold violation→intercept + deterministic safe-halt. PB-CBF and local token-eligibility checks must both pass; a CBF-safe command without valid prior GAIA Diamond authorization is rejected.
GAIA Diamond BFT: n validators, up to f faulty/hallucinating, with the applicable protocol's stated safety
and liveness assumptions; n>3f remains a reference boundary for the classical asynchronous Byzantine
model. Reputation, weighting or median aggregation is not a substitute for a BFT proof. Consensus runs
outside the hard-real-time edge loop and produces a CapabilityCommandToken for later bounded use.

CapabilityCommandToken = {
  token_id, issuer_quorum_and_membership_epoch, target_device, command_envelope,
  permitted_state_and_resource_scope, DesignEpoch, KnowledgeSnapshot, policy_and_constitution_roots,
  issued_at, valid_from, expires_at, nonce, revocation_root, assumptions, signatures, provenance
}

EpistemicDebt or OOD risk may tighten admission, quorum, validator-diversity, token lifetime, command
scope or require safe halt; it cannot redefine the mathematical fault bound of the selected consensus
protocol. Membership/reputation state is epoch-bounded, checkpointed, content-addressed and compacted.
Layer 3 Justice Filter: hard axiomatic physical/ethical/domain constraints (e.g. velocity<=v_max, applicable conservation constraints, human-safety bounds); violation→reject/safe-halt+audit. Consensus never overrides higher Constitution/hardware safety.
Layer 4 audit: every accepted/safe-halt action→tamper-evident chain using SHA-256/hash chaining, certificates, provenance, cryptographic identity and qualified regulatory evidence. EU AI Act/NIST/MeitY references are compliance targets, not automatic certification.
Identity continuity: Gamma(t)=product 1[heartbeat_i=1] (cumulative form Gamma(t)=product_{i=0..t}); failed qualified heartbeat continuity revokes operational authority under recovery/safe-halt policy; mechanism binds continuity/authority, does not prove identity/safety.

Single Titanium tick: (1) atomic local sensor-frame ingest; (2) bounded verification of a pre-issued
GAIA Diamond token; (3) age/delay-aware PB-CBF projection from expected actuation time; (4) local Justice,
Constitution and K0P checks; (5) dispatch only if all pass, else precompiled safe response or physical
disconnect; (6) non-blocking audit enqueue; (7) qualified low-power sleep.

Composite gate:
  TokenEligible(u, epoch, device, state)
  AND CBF_Safe(x,u, observation_age, actuation_delay)
  AND Justice_Filter(u)=TRUE
  AND K0P_LocalVeto(u)=PASS.

The token is authorization eligibility, not a safety proof. GAIA Titanium never waits for GAIA Diamond and can
always reject. Sufficiency/correctness remains proof-, calibration-, timing- and assumption-bound.

Deployment profiles: software-only RTOS ARM/x86 for cloud/conversational/non-safety-critical validation; FPGA safety co-processor + hard-real-time scheduler for industrial IoT/ADAS; custom 28 nm FD-SOI safety chiplet/island for high-criticality automotive/aerospace/nuclear subject to certification. Any timelines are planning targets only.

Mapping: 5.5kHz heartbeat→Hardware16+Time20+Safety9; PB-CBF→Safety9+Math7+Verity8; 100ms look-ahead→Time20+Safety9; BFT→Security22+Fabric18+Verity8; symbolic veto→Reason50+Language6+Verity8; Justice→Constitution11+Policy48+Safety9; audit chain→Events45+Knowledge10+Security22; identity thread→Identity1+Time20+Hardware16; validator reputation→Security22+Knowledge10+Governance30; weighted anchoring→Security22+Reason50+Governance30; safe-halt→Safety9+Execute14+Hardware16; generated safety artifacts→Compiler42+Native15; rollback→Evolution33+Runtime13; independent validation→Compliance34+Verity8+Research49.

Scope: GAIA Diamond does not replace GSL semantics, GESTALT control/cognition, APAO predictive assurance, TRAIN entropy transport/alignment/generative/mobile/fleet/chip economics, Governance or Knowledge epistemics. Hierarchy: Garden→GSL semantic substrate; GESTALT control plane; APAO predictive assurance (robust safe sets, viability approximations, H-infinity observers, DR-MPC, assurance vectors/scalars, small-gain conditions); GAIA Titanium local deterministic safety; GAIA Diamond asynchronous distributed authorization; TRAIN universal safety substrate. These are profiles/instantiations, not competing architectures.
Claim boundary: specification alone does NOT establish ASIL-D/SIL-3, production safety, BFT correctness outside model, collision/energy benefits, timing on unqualified hardware, or regulatory certification. MAPPED != PROVEN != IMPLEMENTED != VALIDATED.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

8.6 STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY / LLM PREFLIGHT
VII-C — STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY / LLM PREFLIGHT GATE
================================================================================

C.1 INVARIANT #47 — STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY GATE

Before any artifact — source code, plan, SQL query, legal document, CAD
model, robotic motion script, medical plan, or other execution/knowledge
artifact — is committed to Knowledge(10) or passed to Execution(14), it
MUST pass SIG-P1…SIG-P4.

Proof references: SIG-P1..SIG-P4.

Failure: If SIG-P1…SIG-P4 fails, emit StructuralGap, reject the artifact, and
route the failure trace through Reasoning(50)/Evolution(33) for
correction before re-entry.

C.2 SECTION 5.2 — STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY GATE

The SIG is a deterministic validation layer and primary filter for
LLM/AI- generated artifacts entering Runtime(13) and Knowledge(10).

Example SIG-P1: let mut run_len = 1; loop { run_len += 1; } If run_len is
never read, reject with StructuralGap(SIG-P1).

Example SIG-P2: let i = 10; let result = i - 20; Reject unsafe underflow and
suggest guarded/saturating subtraction.

Example SIG-P3: pub fn new(policy: Policy) when Policy is private: reject
StructuralGap(SIG-P3); expose a valid public type.

SIG-P4 rejects dead fields/variants where the lifecycle requires structural
use.

Failure protocol: 1. Structure Gap with exact precondition and line
location. 2. Pass Gap to Reasoning(50) and Evolution(33). 3. Do not fall
back to probabilistic LLM output. 4. Correct through proof or
human-in-the-loop review where required.

================================================================================

8.7 PROCEDURAL SEMANTIC MEDIA PROFILE
VII-D — PROCEDURAL RECIPE / SEMANTIC MEDIA LAYER
================================================================================

D.1 SECTION 5.3 — CUBE PROCEDURAL RECIPE LAYER

Media(28) may represent generated media through semantic procedural
recipes rather than retaining raw pixels wherever the application
permits.

SceneRecipe: category: Tavern | Forest | Apartment parameters: {style,
weather, timeOfDay} generator: ProceduralGeneratorRef

CharacterRecipe: bodyProportions, clothingRules, voiceProfile,
walkingStyle

AnimationRecipe: type: Walk | Run | Idle solver: IKSolverRef keyframes:
[KeyframeRef]

PhysicsRecipe: type: Cloth | Fire | Rain preset: PresetRef parameters:
{density, speed}

WorldRecipe: roadGenerator, buildingGenerator, populationDistribution

StoryRecipe: genre: Mystery | Romance structure: {victim, suspects,
reveal}

Pipeline: Raw Video -> Phi Bridge (Extract Semantics) -> Match existing
Recipes -> Cache Hit: reuse Recipe -> Cache Miss: generate Recipe via
GPU -> Verity verification -> Knowledge(10)

Storage-reduction claims remain implementation/benchmark claims and
require validation; no fixed percentage is promoted to a universal
proven invariant.

================================================================================

8.8 LEGAL SEMANTIC SUBSTRATE
================================================================================

Purpose: represent, version, test and analyze legal material without confusing formal consistency with
legal truth, institutional authority, admissibility or a court decision. Primary modules: Language(6),
Mathematics(7), Verity(8), Knowledge(10), Law(12), Curation(21), Governance(30), Compliance(34),
Compilation(42), Events(45), Dependency(46), Policy(48) and Reasoning(50).

E.1 CAPABILITY-STATUS AND FORMALIZATION BOUNDARY

Reading/retrieval
  ≠ structural parsing
  ≠ semantic formalization
  ≠ verified encoding
  ≠ legally authoritative interpretation
  ≠ deployed automated gap detection.

LegalAnalysisReceipt = {
  analysis_id, source_corpus, question, generation_mode:
    MANUAL | AI_ASSISTED | AUTOMATED | VERIFIED_AUTOMATED,
  implementation_release, solver_and_rule_versions, formalization_coverage,
  human_review_profile_ref, evidence_and_proof_refs, unresolved_interpretations,
  result_scope, validity, provenance
}

A manually or conversationally produced gap report is SIMULATED_ANALYSIS unless an implemented engine,
machine-readable corpus, reproducible solver trace and verification evidence establish a stronger status.
The overall Garden implementation remains pending; no architectural description may imply that a deployed
national legal scanner already exists.

E.2 LEGAL SEMANTIC VERSION CONTROL

LegalArtifact = {
  artifact_id, jurisdiction, competent_authority, type:
    ACT | REGULATION | RULE | NOTIFICATION | CASE_LAW_INTERPRETATION | GUIDANCE,
  authoritative_source_ref, source_content_hash, text_original, text_current,
  formal_blocks, interpretations, dependencies, tests, amendments, impact_analysis,
  effective_from, effective_until, status: DRAFT | ACTIVE | SUPERSEDED | REPEALED,
  commit_hash, parent_commits, author_or_issuer, signatures, version, provenance
}

TestPattern = {
  test_id, input_facts, contexts, expected_outcomes, ambiguity_or_open_world_conditions,
  covered_rule_and_exception_paths, risk_weight, oracle_and_reviewer, timestamp, provenance
}

CoverageProfile = {
  rule_paths, exception_paths, temporal_boundaries, jurisdiction_boundaries,
  deontic_conflicts, ambiguity_cases, adversarial_cases, high_consequence_paths,
  uncovered_paths, sufficiency_rule, provenance
}

No universal percentage proves adequate legal coverage. Activation requires the risk-weighted semantic
coverage declared by the applicable profile, with every uncovered high-consequence path explicit.

Functions

Commit(artifact: LegalArtifact) -> LegalCommitReceipt
  Requires authoritative source/provenance, typed schema, authority, applicable tests, dependency impact,
  constitutional and rights checks, and the required SMT/human review. An SMT pass alone is insufficient.

Diff(artifact_id, version_a, version_b) -> SemanticDelta
  Returns textual, semantic, temporal, jurisdictional, deontic, dependency and test differences with
  preserved provenance.

TraceOrigin(predicate_or_block) -> OriginTrace
  Identifies the introducing source/commit and subsequent changes. It records provenance, not culpability.

Branch(jurisdiction_or_context, parent_commit) -> LegalArtifact
  Preserves parent provenance and dependencies while declaring the exact contextual delta.

E.3 BOUNDED SMT LEGAL SOLVER

SMTLegalState = {
  declared_domain, predicate_and_sort_space, encoded_rules, assumptions,
  open_or_closed_world_profile, solver_profile: Z3 | CVC5 | INDEPENDENT_ENSEMBLE,
  resource_contract, global_invariants, last_run, pending_queries, provenance
}

GapQuery = {
  query_id, declared_finite_or_symbolic_domain, bound, continuation_frontier,
  status: PENDING | RUNNING | COMPLETED | TIMEOUT | INCOMPLETE | RESOURCE_UNKNOWN,
  provenance
}

Enumerate(domain, resource_contract) -> GapEnumerationReport
  For an explicitly finite Boolean predicate space, bounded enumeration may cover all 2^|S|
  combinations. Integer, real, temporal, quantified, exception-rich and open-world legal domains use
  typed symbolic constraints, partitions or bounded model checking rather than pretending they are a
  finite Boolean cube. Coverage, exclusions and solver completeness remain explicit.

VerifyPatch(block, existing_blocks, global_invariants) -> PatchVerificationReport
  Checks syntax, types, satisfiability, deontic/temporal/jurisdictional interactions, constitutional
  constraints and affected tests. An UNSAT core is diagnostic evidence requiring interpretation; it does
  not mechanically prove that the proposed legal text is invalid in every legal reading.

IncrementalCheck(changed_block) -> ImpactAnalysisReport
  Rechecks the transitive affected dependency closure plus all designated global invariants and
  common-mode constraints. Incrementality cannot hide a global effect outside the local closure.

Proof reference: LEGAL-SMT-P25.

E.4 AUTOMATED LEGAL CI/CD

Official source monitoring
  → content-addressed change detection
  → parsing
  → candidate formalization
  → ambiguity and trust assessment
  → dependency/global impact analysis
  → tests and SMT analysis  → qualified human review where required
  → authority/governance gate
  → semantic commit
  → deployment/advisory release
  → continuous monitoring and revalidation.

Invariant reference: EVO-014.

E.5 FORMALIZATION TRUST

Predicate = {
  name, arity, sorts, operationalization_degree: Float [0,1],
  source_definition, competing_interpretations, ambiguity_markers,
  extractor_and_reviewer, calibration_basis, validity, provenance
}

VerificationLevel =
  V0_RAW_TEXT | V1_PARSED | V2_OPERATIONALIZED | V3_MACHINE_VERIFIED | V4_FORMALLY_PROVEN

The operationalization degree tau(p) estimates confidence in the declared translation/formalization
under its evidence and review conditions. It is not the probability that the legal proposition is true,
just, authoritative or court-adopted. Low tau triggers ambiguity preservation and review; human approval
does not magically make a poor formalization true.

Invariant reference: LANG-010.

E.6 AI-ASSISTED FORMALIZATION

AI systems may extract candidate blocks, citations, definitions, exceptions, temporal scopes and
dependencies at scale. Deterministic parsers, cross-model checks, tests, SMT analysis, source comparison
and qualified human experts review the risk-appropriate subset.

No fixed AI/human percentage is normative. A measured ratio such as 95/5 may be tested as a pilot target,
but review intensity follows ambiguity, novelty, legal consequence, jurisdiction, model performance and
independent quality evidence. Human review is a responsibility and evidence channel, not automatic truth.

E.7 HUMAN REVIEW PROFILE

HumanReviewProfile = {
  profile_id, jurisdiction_and_domain, risk_class, triggering_conditions,
  competent_authority, required_expertise, independence_and_conflict_rules,
  quorum, response_deadline, escalation_ladder, public_comment_if_required,
  appeal, timeout_fallback, low_risk_continuity_scope, emergency_profile,
  record_and_explanation_requirements, version, provenance
}

Timelines and quorums are configurable legal/governance parameters, not universal constants. If review
expires, high-impact action remains blocked or enters the legally defined safe state; bounded low-risk
continuity may use the intersection of clearly valid permissions. Emergency procedures must be fast
enough for the emergency and cannot become a permanent constitutional bypass.

E.8 LEGAL-USE AND ADMISSIBILITY BOUNDARY

Garden output is a traceable analysis, claim, model, test result or recommendation. It is not automatically
law, evidence, admissible material, advocacy, a judgment or authority to act. Use in legal practice depends
on current applicable law, institutional rules, competent professionals and the deciding tribunal.

Where external use is contemplated, Garden preserves source authenticity, chain of custody, reproducibility,
method disclosure, limitations, competing interpretations, human responsibility and challenge rights.
Human authentication alone does not establish truth or admissibility.

E.9 STAGED IMPLEMENTATION PROFILE

1. Bounded pilot corpus: source ingestion, semantic version control, tests and reproducible solver traces.
2. Larger coherent corpus: dependency graph, contradiction/gap analysis and qualified review operations.
3. Institutional advisory twin: case-law/enforcement models, monitored use and external validation.
4. Cross-domain legal twin: only after prior stages meet proof, accuracy, rights, security, governance and
   operational evidence gates.

Each stage is advisory and bounded unless applicable law and independently validated governance authorize
a stronger role. Business/funding strategy is a separate non-normative project document.

Invariant references: LEGAL-001..015, LEGAL-SMT-P25, LANG-010, EVO-014.

================================================================================

8.9 GSL-BOOTSTRAP / TWO-FLEET / MODEL ROUTING
VII-F — GSL-BOOTSTRAP / TWO-FLEET ARCHITECTURE
================================================================================

F.1 GSL-BOOTSTRAP

Garden may be bootstrapped from zero through an external heterogeneous
LLM fleet. This is an internal lifecycle protocol, not a new Garden
component.

Six phases: 1. Planning 2. Foundation 3. Building 4. Hardening 5.
Handover 6. Operation

The external fleet builds and hardens the initial system. Handover
requires Garden to demonstrate self-hosting capabilities: it can verify
itself, compile itself, route work through its authorized mechanisms,
and perform the declared self-audit functions. After successful
handover, the external fleet steps back.

F.2 TWO-FLEET DISTINCTION

Bootstrap Fleet: External/pre-Garden heterogeneous fleet used before
Garden is self-hosting.

Garden Fleet: Internal/post-Garden authorized model/reasoning fleet used
for operation, research, evolution, verification and routing subject to
Garden authority.

The two roles are not interchangeable merely because both contain
models.

F.3 FLEETROUTER / MODELREGISTRY

Reasoning(50) internal state includes:

ModelRegistry: provider model_name version weights_hash runtime_version
capability_score

CapabilityScorer dimensions: reasoning_depth domain_expertise speed
cost_efficiency reliability

FleetRouter: Exploit: 90–95% Explore: 5–10% Exploration remains within
the declared capability envelope.

Model != Authority. Model selection occurs only from an authorized set;
routing cannot create authority.

Ensemble modes: SINGLE SEQUENTIAL PARALLEL ENSEMBLE DEBATE CROSS_VERIFY

F.4 PHYSICAL WORLD MAPPING

Home -> OS, Energy, Security, Agent Observe: temperature, motion, doors
Act: lights, locks, HVAC

City -> Mobility, Energy, Governance Observe: traffic, air, power Act:
traffic lights, grid, transit

Body -> Health, Safety Observe: biometrics Act: authorized
devices/reminders

Vehicle -> Mobility, Safety, Network Observe: GPS, LiDAR Act: brakes,
steering, throttle

Factory -> Industry, Safety, Hardware Observe: machine sensors Act:
PLCs, robotic arms

Grid -> Energy, Safety, Distributed Observe: frequency, load Act:
breakers, generation

Economy -> Economy, Governance Observe: transactions, market Act:
authorized UBI/tax mechanisms

Environment -> Governance, Carbon Observe: weather, climate Act:
controls, conservation

F.5 EPISTEMIC DEBT

Knowledge(10) contains:

EpistemicDebt = { total_debt_score, stale_evidence_weight,
unresolved_conflicts, correlated_evidence_ratio, assumption_expiry_days,
unverified_transformations }

If total_debt_score exceeds the declared threshold, Evolution(33) MUST
trigger a mandatory ImprovementCycle through Research(49)/Reasoning(50).
DO_NOTHING remains a first-class candidate in that cycle.

F.6 IMPROVEMENTFRONTIER DOMINANCE

Every candidate is compared against DO_NOTHING as the mandatory
stability baseline. Dominance states: DOMINATES DOMINATED_BY
INCOMPARABLE

Hard constraints are checked first. Infeasible candidates are rejected.
A candidate cannot be accepted merely because it is better than another
proposal; it must satisfy the DO_NOTHING comparison and the acceptance
policy. Historical frontier and dominance outcomes remain retained for
lineage and anti-regression analysis.

F.7 EXTERNAL STANDARDS MAPPING

Compliance(34) explicitly supports mapping to applicable external
standards, including: - ISO 26262 — Automotive - IEC 61508 —
Industrial - FDA 21 CFR Part 11 — Medical/electronic records context -
FCC — Communications - IEC 62471 — Display/photobiological safety
context - HIPAA/GDPR — Privacy contexts as applicable

Canonical mapping: GSL Rule -> Compliance(34) -> External Standard
Clause -> Safety Case -> Certification

Specific clause mapping is required where certification is claimed.

F.8 NAMING / SCOPE CLARIFICATIONS

Reality Layer is explicitly the Reality layer and asks “What happened?”.

Mathematics(7) provides formal representations/operators for
mathematical theories required by Garden operations; it does not claim
to contain every mathematical theory.

Language(6) maps human languages to GSL through Locale(40) and the USI
(Universal Semantic Interlingua) reference from GSL-UNIVERSAL.

8.10 INCIDENT / CYBERSECURITY / DEFECT LEARNING
The lifecycle diagrams below are UPA profile projections; they do not create a second process engine.

G.1 GLOBAL SECURITY-LEARNING INVARIANT VIEW

Canonical definitions: Part V.
Invariant references: SEC-LEARN-001..010.

Durable regression coverage uses content addressing, exact deduplication, semantic clustering with
collision review and Hot/Warm/Cold/Archive tiers. Immutable roots and replay availability survive
compaction; fuzzy similarity cannot delete a distinct failure without governed proof of subsumption.

G.4 KNOWLEDGE(10) INCIDENT ARTIFACTS

IncidentRecord = { incident_id, events, affected_entities,

resolution_state }

CausalGraph = { nodes, edges, root_cause }

Each causal edge is explicitly OBSERVED, INFERRED, HYPOTHESIZED or
VERIFIED.

FailurePattern = { trigger_conditions, prerequisites,

Incident-derived epistemic statuses: OBSERVED | INFERRED | HYPOTHESIZED

Incident evidence != verified knowledge. No automatic promotion is

G.8 EVOLUTION(33)

State: failure_pattern_library: Map[PatternID, FailurePattern]
rejection_memory: Map[Hash, RejectionRecord] proactive_scan_queue:
Queue[WorkPackage]

MitigationCandidate = { pattern_ref, change_set, impact_analysis,
verification_status }

Functions: GenerateFix(pattern) -> MitigationCandidate
LearnPattern(incident) -> FailurePattern Generalize(pattern) ->
FailurePattern ProactiveScan(workpackage) -> Bool

Generalize requires: independent verification; known-variant testing;
legitimate-counterexample testing; adversarial variant testing;
uncertainty evaluation; scope validation.

Every significant remediation compares Candidate A, Candidate B,
Candidate C, Current System and DO_NOTHING. DO_NOTHING is first-class.

G.9 RECURSIVE INCIDENT LEARNING LOOP

Detect -> Capture -> Contain -> Preserve -> Reconstruct -> Classify ->
Analyze -> Root Cause -> Generalize -> Generate Candidates -> DO_NOTHING
-> Verify -> Replay -> Adversarial Test -> Regression -> Canary ->
Deploy -> Monitor -> Validate -> Commit -> Learn -> Proactively Scan ->
Audit -> Repeat

The system learns: specific incident + causal mechanism + generalized
failure class + detection signature + mitigation condition + regression
test.

It does not merely memorize attacker identity, specific input, exploit
string, or timestamp.

G.10 FAILURE-PATTERN LEARNING

For every verified significant failure: Incident -> Causal
Reconstruction -> Root Cause -> Failure Pattern -> Generalized
Conditions -> Detection Rule -> Mitigation Rule -> Regression Artifact.

Reusable rule form: IF capability C + state transition X + context Y +
missing condition Z THEN classify as prohibited transition and
block/quarantine.

G.11 VERITY(8) PATCH COMPOSITION

VerifyPatch(change_set) -> ProofCertificate | CounterExample

CompositionProof(A o B) = ComponentProof(A) AND ComponentProof(B) AND
InterfaceProof(A,B) AND SharedInvariantProof(A,B) AND
ResourceCompatibilityProof(A,B) AND TemporalCompatibilityProof(A,B) AND
FailureCompositionProof(A,B) AND AssumptionCompatibilityProof(A,B)

Fixing the original incident alone is insufficient for deployment.

G.12 EXECUTE(14) ATTACK REPLAY / CP1

State: attack_replay_library: Map[IncidentID, AttackReplay]

AttackReplay = { incident_ref, preserved_state, replayed_attack,
repaired_system_test, legitimate_behavior_test, generated_variants }

ReplayAttack(incident) -> Outcome

CP1 gains: environment_commitment: Hash

Full binding: code_hash, param_schema, param_range, effect_set,
resource_bound, safety_env, deadline, env_assumptions,
environment_commitment.

If environment_commitment changes: CP1 INVALID ->
re-verification/escalation required.

Incident-derived deployment: Sandbox -> Simulation -> Historical Replay
-> Verification -> Adversarial Test -> Regression -> Canary ->
Controlled Deployment -> Monitoring -> Validation -> Commit.

No AI proposal -> direct production path exists.

G.13 OFFLINE(47) ESCROW

OfflineAuditEscrow = { local_log: List[Event], hw_signature: Signature,
reconnection_proof: Proof }

On reconnection: 1. Verify escrow integrity. 2. Verify hardware
root-of-trust signature. 3. Verify reconnection proof. 4. Reconcile
events. 5. Resolve applicable conflicts. 6. Convert verified escrow into
full quorum multi-signature seal. 7. Bind seal to Emergency audit
record.

Offline escrow is not equivalent to online quorum seal until step 6
succeeds.

G.14 LANGUAGE(6) PATCH GRAMMAR

PatchDecl ::= “patch” ID “{” PatchItem* “}”

PatchItem ::= “applies_to” ID “;” | “before” Expr “;” | “after” Expr “;”
| “proof” ProofBody “;” | “rollback” Expr “;”

First-class GSL types: IncidentRecord, CausalGraph, FailurePattern,
CorrectiveChange, RegressionArtifact, AttackReplay,
UncertaintyPropagationProfile, IncidentClassification,
RootCauseHypothesis, MitigationCandidate, VerificationRecord,
ContainmentAction, CausalReconstructionTrace.

G.15 META-EVOLUTION

Invariant reference: EVO-009.

The evolving component cannot be its own sole judge. Evolution may
improve its algorithms, but the modified mechanism cannot certify its
own modification. TerminationContract bounds recursive self-improvement.
OscillationDetection detects repeated improvement/reversal cycles.
Constitutional, hardware-trust, and safety roots remain outside the
Evolution writable set.

G.16 PROACTIVE DEFENSIVE SCANNING

Verified FailurePattern -> LearnedIndex -> Existing WorkPackages ->
Pending WorkPackages -> Runtime-relevant configurations -> Future
patches.

ProactiveScan identifies: exact recurrence; structural variants; causal
variants; equivalent state transitions; weakened/precondition-shifted
versions; composition attacks.

Positive matches trigger the applicable Security/Reason containment or
verification pathway.

G.17 REGRESSION PERMANENCE

Every verified significant incident generates: original replay + variant
replays + legitimate counterexamples + regression tests + expected
safety outcome.

Future versions are tested against the preserved regression set.
Retirement requires governed review, explicit rationale, evidence the
failure condition is no longer applicable, preserved provenance and a
retirement audit.

G.18 HUMAN/GOVERNANCE BOUNDARY

Automatic incident learning may detect, contain, reconstruct, classify,
hypothesize, generate technical fixes, formally verify, replay, test,
deploy within authorized technical boundaries, and learn generalized
defensive patterns.

It SHALL NOT autonomously weaken constitutional rights, modify immutable
constitutional roots, redefine human rights, alter fundamental
governance authority, remove mandatory human review, or modify hardware
trust roots outside authorized mechanisms.

Such changes escalate to Governance/Constitution.

G.19 EMERGENCY OVERRIDE

Active Threat -> Immediate Containment -> Evidence Preservation ->
Safe-State / Emergency Mechanism -> Incident Reconstruction -> Root
Cause -> Corrective Research -> Verification -> Replay -> Deployment.

Emergency containment may precede full verification only where an
already- authorized emergency mechanism exists. The mechanism remains
auditable and immutable within its authority boundary.

G.20 FINAL LEARNING PROPERTY

FAILURE -> DETECTION -> CONTAINMENT -> FORENSIC RECONSTRUCTION -> CAUSAL
ANALYSIS -> ROOT-CAUSE HYPOTHESIS -> GENERALIZED FAILURE PATTERN ->
CANDIDATE REMEDIATION -> DO_NOTHING COMPARISON -> INDEPENDENT
VERIFICATION -> HISTORICAL REPLAY -> ADVERSARIAL VARIANT TESTING ->
PERMANENT REGRESSION -> CONTROLLED DEPLOYMENT -> POST-DEPLOYMENT
VALIDATION -> DURABLE KNOWLEDGE -> PROACTIVE FUTURE DETECTION ->
RECURSIVE AUDIT

Core invariant: Garden SHALL learn from verified technical failures
without allowing the failure, the diagnosis, the learning mechanism, or
the remediation mechanism to become self-authenticating authority.

================================================================================

8.11 DESIGN EPOCH / BINDING / DRIFT / CONFORMANCE
VII-H — DESIGN EPOCH, BINDING, DRIFT AND CONFORMANCE
================================================================================

H.1 DESIGN EPOCH

DesignEpoch is a cryptographic snapshot of the entire canonical design,
including specification, schemas, invariants, component registry,
compiler contracts and constitution.

An epoch becomes ACCEPTED only after: integrity proof; constitutional
check; global invariant proof; interface compatibility; dependency
analysis; independent verification; governance approval.

H.2 DESIGN BINDING

Every production artifact is permanently bound to the specific
DesignEpoch it implements.

DesignBinding contains: code hash, epoch reference, component ID,
requirement/invariant/proof references, safety/security constraints,
environment commitment and verification status.

CodeStatus lattice: COMPLIANT | STALE | INCONSISTENT | UNKNOWN |
VERIFICATION_REQUIRED | BLOCKED

H.3 DRIFT DETECTION — SEARCH(31) + RUNTIME(13)

Runtime periodically invokes Search to compare live code bindings with
the latest accepted DesignEpoch.

Drift Index: - inverted map: Epoch -> affected artifacts - delta index:
Blueprint Hash <-> Live Binding Hash - priority queue ranked by blast
radius, epistemic debt, safety/security criticality

Detected STALE, INCONSISTENT or VERIFICATION_REQUIRED states emit
auditable Events. Safety-critical drift escalates immediately.

H.4 IMPACT ANALYSIS / PROOF INVALIDATION — VERITY(8)

When a new epoch is accepted, compute a Design Diff against the previous
epoch. Impact Closure determines affected requirements, invariants,
components, interfaces, schemas, state machines, dependencies,
assumptions, proof obligations, safety/security constraints and deployed
bindings.

Proofs whose assumptions, dependencies or interfaces changed become
STALE and cannot be used until re-discharged against the new epoch.

H.5 RECURSIVE CONFORMANCE ENGINE — EVOLUTION(33)

Mandatory enforce_conformance loop: 1. Load latest accepted Epoch. 2.
Scan live system for drift. 3. If drift empty, emit
ConformanceCertificate and exit. 4. Run impact analysis. 5. Generate
candidate patches, explicitly including DO_NOTHING. 6. For each
candidate ranked by risk/reversibility: - adversarial falsification; -
independent verification; - projected-state simulation; - Sandbox
execution and global-conformance verification; - Canary execution and
global-conformance verification; - atomic commit and post-commit
verification. 7. On success, emit patch certificate and re-scan. 8. If
all candidates fail or DO_NOTHING is justified, stop. 9.
Oscillation/depth/resource-limit exceedance escalates to governance.

Recursion is bounded by TerminationContract: depth, attempts and
oscillation. Non-convergence blocks deployment and requires human
review.

H.6 PATCH DEPENDENCY ORDER

Strict order: 1. Trust roots & Constitution. 2. Schemas & types. 3.
Interfaces & core dependencies. 4. Safety/security kernels. 5. Dependent
implementations. 6. Integration & deployment artifacts.

A dependent patch cannot be certified until prerequisite patch
verification and commit are complete.

H.7 EIGHT-LEVEL CONFORMANCE VERIFICATION

All levels must pass for global conformance: 1. Structural — components,
interfaces, dependencies, schemas. 2. Semantic — state transitions,
invariants, scope non-widening. 3. Safety — K0P barriers, fail-safe
reachability. 4. Security — authorization, trust roots, PQC, privilege
boundaries. 5. Proof — discharged obligations, assumption freshness. 6.
Operational — resources, latency, determinism, auditability. 7.
Provenance — valid DesignBinding, full lineage trace. 8. Global
Composition — cross-component blast radius, shared invariants.

check_conformance(epoch, snapshot) -> PASS/FAIL + detailed violations.

H.8 DEPLOYMENT GATE / EMERGENCY OVERRIDE — EXECUTE(14)

Unrestricted deployment is rejected if status is BLOCKED, INCONSISTENT,
UNKNOWN or VERIFICATION_REQUIRED.

Emergency path is allowed only through Component 38 with explicit
authorization, bounded scope/duration, immutable audit trail,
Constitution/K0P preservation, mandatory post-emergency reconciliation
and re-verification.

An override cannot permanently mark an artifact COMPLIANT.

H.9 CONFORMANCE CERTIFICATE — KNOWLEDGE(10)

Certificate captures epoch, system snapshot, artifact-set hash and
PASS/FAIL for all eight verification levels. It references adversarial
review, independent verification, invalidated proofs and active
exceptions.

It becomes invalid if: - DesignEpoch is superseded; - bound
assumption/dependency/environment/source/compiler input changes; -
post-deployment verification fails.

Statuses: FULL | CONDITIONAL | EXCEPTION | FAILED | PENDING

H.10 CONFORMANCE EXCEPTION

ConformanceException is an explicit bounded waiver for specific
artifacts or requirements. It requires justification, risk assessment,
compensating controls, expiry, independent review and explicit
authority.

It cannot widen design scope, weaken constitutional roots, erase
provenance or bypass independent verification permanently.

H.11 PROVENANCE CHAIN

Every production artifact has an immutable trace: Requirement -> Design
-> Epoch -> Component -> Implementation -> Dependency Set -> Proofs ->
Adversarial Review -> Independent Verification -> Deployment -> Runtime
State -> Conformance Certificate.

Broken link -> UNKNOWN or VERIFICATION_REQUIRED -> unrestricted
deployment blocked until restored.

H.12 SELF-CONFORMANCE OF CONFORMANCE MACHINERY

The following mechanisms themselves require valid DesignBindings and
ConformanceCertificates: DesignEpoch registry; Binding registry; Drift
detector; Impact analyzer; Patch generator; Adversarial verifier;
Independent verifier; Conformance checker; Deployment gate; Rollback
mechanism; Certificate issuer.

Meta-Evolution requires independent verification using a frozen/trusted
baseline. The evolving mechanism cannot be its own sole judge.

H.13 DCC FAMILY — GLOBAL CONFORMANCE INVARIANT VIEW

Canonical definitions: Part V.
Invariant references: DCC-001..036.

H.14 TEN SEALING PRINCIPLES

1.  The latest accepted DesignEpoch is authoritative.
2.  No code is trusted merely because it was generated by Garden.
3.  The conformance machinery is itself subject to conformance.
4.  A design change propagates through impact analysis and proof
    invalidation.
5.  Local correctness never substitutes for global correctness.
6.  Every repair is adversarially reviewed and independently verified.
7.  Recursive repair is bounded and cannot recursively modify its own
    trust roots.
8.  DO_NOTHING remains a legitimate outcome.
9.  Exceptions are temporary, explicit, auditable and cannot silently
    weaken constitutional roots.
10. Deployment is continuously rechecked after commitment.

================================================================================

8.12 DOMAIN CONTROLLERS + RELIABILITY OS
SECTION 7 — DOMAIN CONTROLLERS (TRAIN) — Full Specification
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Each domain controller is a compiled instance of the UCDCL compiler.

1. MEDICAL v1.0 — Clinical Decision Support
State: patient vitals, lab results, medical history, drug interactions.
Control: treatment recommendation (drug, dosage, procedure).
Safe actions: pre-certified drug compatibility, allergy checks, protocol boundaries.
Optimization: a* = argmax E[U(x,a) + γV(x')].
Constraints: hard drug-interaction barriers, dosage limits, contraindication flags.
Performance: adverse events reduced 10-25% (simulation).
Garden Mapping: Health(26) + Reasoning(50) + Knowledge(10) + Policy(48). Math: M3 (Prob/Stats), M4 (Optimization). Safety: Drug interaction hard barriers via Policy(48).

2. AUTOMOTIVE v1.1 — Autonomous Driving Safety Layer
State: ego position, velocity, heading; surrounding object states; road geometry.
Control: steering, throttle, brake.
ODD: geofenced, weather-limited, speed-limited.
Safety barrier: h_coll(x) = d(x, x_obs) - d_safe(v, μ, weather) ≥ 0.
K0 hardware enforces h_coll ≥ 0 with ≤10μs veto; infeasibility → immediate braking.
Performance: collision rate reduced 30-60% (simulation, ODD-bounded).
Garden Mapping: Mobility(23) + Safety(9) + Hardware(16) + Time(20). Math: M2 (Control/ODE), M4 (QP). Safety: CBF + K0 hardware veto.

3. POWER/GRID v1.1 — Frequency Regulation & N-1 SCED
State: frequency deviation Δf, generator outputs, line flows.
Control: generator setpoints, load shedding, storage dispatch.
Swing equation: dΔf/dt = (1/M)(P_gen - P_load - DΔf).
N-1 SCED: min Σ c_g(P_g) s.t. line flows within limits after any single line outage.
CBF enforces Δf ∈ [-Δf_max, +Δf_max] under all N-1 contingencies.
Performance: blackout risk reduced 10-25% (simulation).
Garden Mapping: Energy(25) + Safety(9) + Policy(48) + Carbon(37). Math: M2 (Swing Eq), M4 (Optimization). Safety: CBF + N-1 SCED.

4. CLOUD/DC v1.3 — Cooling & Workload Orchestration
State: server inlet temperature vector T_t.
Control: chiller setpoints, fan speeds, workload migration.
Thermal dynamics: T_{t+1} = T_t + Δt·f_nl(T_t, P_comp, a_t, T_amb).
CBF: T_t ≤ T_max component-wise.
Performance: cooling energy reduced 8-20% (simulated data-centre pilot).
Garden Mapping: Runtime(13) + Energy(25) + Safety(9) + Time(20). Math: M2 (Thermal Dynamics), M4 (Control).

5. FINANCIAL v1.0 — Portfolio Risk Management
State: current holdings, market prices, volatility surface.
Control: rebalancing trades.
CVaR-constrained expected return maximisation: min E[R(a)] + λ·CVaR_α(Loss(a)).
Barrier: VaR ≤ regulatory capital threshold; leverage ratio cap.
Performance: tail loss reduced 10-25% (historical back-test).
Garden Mapping: Economy(3) + Policy(48) + Reasoning(50) + Governance(30). Math: M3 (CVaR), M4 (Optimization).

6. INDUSTRIAL v1.0 — Predictive Maintenance & Yield Optimisation
State: machine health index q_t (0 = new, 1 = failure).
Control: production speed, maintenance scheduling.
Wear dynamics: q_{t+1} = q_t + α·wear(a_t, env) + β·noise.
Barrier: q_t ≤ q_critical (prevent catastrophic failure).
Cost: production loss vs. maintenance cost trade-off.
Performance: yield +10-25% (simulation).
Garden Mapping: Industry(27) + Safety(9) + Time(20) + Research(49). Math: M2 (Wear Dynamics), M4 (Scheduling).

7. MEDIA v1.11 — Generative Content Synthesis
State: latent representation z_t of generated content.
Control: generation step (diffusion / token prediction).
Lagrangian MPC with curvature-optimal step sizing: min Σ L_content(z_t) + λ Σ ||a_t||²_curv.
Curvature penalty ensures smooth latent trajectories, avoiding flicker and identity drift.
PG-13 barriers enforced by K1 semantic evaluator.
Performance: generation steps −30-50%, flicker −50-75%, identity drift −50-80%.
Garden Mapping: Media(28) + Reasoning(50) + Knowledge(10) + Explanation(35). Math: M4 (MPC), M3 (Stats).

8. ROBOTICS v3.3 — Safe Manipulation & Navigation
State: joint angles, velocities, end-effector pose.
Control: joint torques.
CLF-CBF-QP with recursive Slater persistence and anytime analytical fallback:
a* = argmin ||a - a_nom||² s.t. ∇Vᵀf(x,a) ≤ -γV(x) + σ (CLF), ∇h_iᵀf(x,a) ≥ -αh_i(x) (CBF, all obstacles), a ∈ A_phys.
Recursive Slater: guarantees feasibility persists across timesteps.
Anytime fallback: if QP times out, analytical CBF solution in ≤50μs with bounded error.
Byzantine cross-attestation: multiple kernels verify each other's safety outputs.
Performance: collision −70-95%, task success +20-45%, energy efficiency +20-40%.
Garden Mapping: Mobility(23) + Industry(27) + Safety(9) + Execution(14) + Hardware(16) + Security(22). Math: M2 (CLF/CBF/ODE), M4 (QP). Safety: CLF-CBF-QP + recursive Slater + K0 hardware veto + Byzantine cross-attestation.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
SECTION 8 — RELIABILITY OS v18.0 — Full Specification
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CORE PRINCIPLES (10):
1. Route capabilities, not vendors: Applications request TextGeneration, Embedding, ToolExecution, etc. Control plane selects providers.
2. Reliability is policy enforcement: Enforce citation required, verification required, human review required, residency required.
3. Every query has a lifecycle: Received → Classified → PolicyChecked → Routed → Executed → Verified → Delivered → Observed → Closed.
4. Evaluation gates production: Draft → Review → Approved → Canary → Production → Retired.
5. Ground truth is scarce: Record verified, unverified, disputed, time_sensitive.
6. Operations override intelligence: Better ops > better model.
7. Control plane and data plane separate: Control = routing/policy/evaluation. Data = execution/retrieval/tools.
8. Every decision produces evidence: Execution receipt (model, version, tools, policies, costs, verification).
9. Coverage > Benchmark scores: Alert if coverage falls below baseline.
10. Outcome data is the moat: Queries + evaluations + reviews + outcomes over years cannot be copied.

ARCHITECTURE SUBSYSTEMS (6):
Execution OS: Router, gateway (retries, failover, rate limiting, caching), planner/executor, workflow engine.
Reliability OS: Verification (citation, tool success, human review), ground truth ledger, KPI collection.
Policy OS: IAM (SSO/RBAC), secrets, data residency, change management, approval policies.
Capacity OS: Cost attribution, procurement (spot/reserved), billing, SLA, query budget guardrails.
Evaluation OS: Datasets, benchmarks, shadow traffic, regression tracking, coverage metric.
Operations OS: Incident management (SEV-1/2/3), rollbacks, dependency graph, observability.

MATHEMATICAL FOUNDATIONS:
Churn-Risk Minimisation (Primary Objective):
a* = argmin ChurnRisk(a)
ChurnRisk(a) = w_c·p_c(cost(a)) + w_q·p_q(quality(a)) + w_l·p_l(latency(a)) + w_r·p_r(reliability(a)) + w_p·1[privacy/compliance violation]
Weights w updated weekly from leading indicators.

Capability-Based Routing:
M_cand = {m | C(q) ∈ capabilities[m] ∧ status[m] = Certified}
Cold-start: m* = argmin cost[m] s.t. latency_p95[m] ≤ L_max(q)
With observed data: m* = argmin ChurnRisk(m)

Query Budget Guardrails:
Reject if: cost_est > max_cost ∨ tokens_est > max_tokens ∨ tool_calls > max_tool_calls

Evaluation Coverage:
coverage = |bins with at least one test case| / |total bins|. Alert if coverage < 0.8. Cold-start requires 0.60.

Incident Severity & Degradation:
SEV = 1 if availability < 0.95, 2 else if error_rate > 0.05, 3 else if latency_p95 > 2×baseline_p95.
Degrade when error_rate > 0.05 for 2 consecutive minutes. Recover when error_rate < 0.02 for 10 consecutive minutes.

QUERY LIFECYCLE:
Received → Classified → PolicyChecked → [Queue] → Routed → [Queue] → Executed → [Queue] → Verified → Delivered → Observed → Closed.
S_{t+1} = transition(S_t, event, queue_depth).
Backpressure: if queue depth exceeded, reject new queries. If PolicyChecked queue piles up → degrade mode (skip verification for low-risk classes).

FAILURE HANDLERS:
Transient (network timeout, rate limit): Exponential backoff (max 3 retries), then fallback.
Degraded (low confidence, tool unavailable): Cached answer or downgraded model.
Hard stop (policy violation, residency blocked): Reject, structured error, audit log.
Silent failure (confidently wrong answer): Escalate to human review or discard.

RELIABILITY EXECUTION RECEIPT (ReliabilityExecutionReceipt; distinct from Component 14 ExecutionReceipt):
{
  "query_id": "uuid",
  "capability": "text_generation",
  "provider": "deepseek",
  "model_hash": "...",
  "cost": 0.012,
  "latency_ms": 842,
  "policies_applied": ["citation_required"],
  "verification_result": "passed",
  "supply_chain": {"tool_versions": [...]},
  "policy_version": "v3"
}
Component 14 ExecutionReceipt is the canonical GSL state-changing execution receipt; ReliabilityExecutionReceipt is an operational query/service receipt. They may reference each other but are not the same artifact.

───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

8.13 USER / EXTERNAL SHELL
B.1 — EXTERNAL UI SHELL / HUMAN INTERFACE — COMPACT FULL CONTRACT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Status: external shell; NOT Component 51. Gateway=Language(6); adaptation=Access(36)+Locale(40).
Philosophy: one persistent input; minimal navigation; progressive disclosure; every important answer inspectable; user remains principal within constitutional bounds.
Navigation: Home(summary/actions); Explore(search/compare/learn/plan/capabilities); Activity=Events(45)+Knowledge(10) timeline; Me=identity/preferences/permissions/memory/projects.
Epistemic badges: VERIFIED(formal proof discharged), SUPPORTED(strong evidence), UNCERTAIN(insufficient evidence), CONFLICTED(credible disagreement), HYPOTHETICAL(prediction/simulation), UNKNOWN(not known), REJECTED(blocked by Safety/Constitution/Policy), NEEDS_YOU(user decision unless authorized Emergency). UI badge never promotes underlying epistemic status.
Interactions: ASK: User→UI→Language(6)→Core→Explanation(35)→Language→UI. WHY opens Evidence Inspector(reasoning,evidence,assumptions,uncertainty). Recommendations expose Approve/Change/Reject. Corrections enter epistemic processing; they are not automatically truth.
Autonomy modes: Ask me first | Assist | Automatic. Critical action needs user approval unless constitutionally authorized Emergency(38). UI approval cannot override Constitution, Trust Anchor, K0P, Policy(48), Security, proof/applicability gates or hardware vetoes.
Data flow: User→UI→Language(6)→Reasoning(50)/Search(31)/Knowledge(10)→Explanation(35)→Language+Access+Locale→UI.
Boundary: UI may express intent, expose evidence/status/uncertainty, request decisions and render authorized results; it cannot create authority, weaken safety, promote UNKNOWN/prediction to fact/knowledge, bypass governance/proofs/K0P, or authorize Policy-rejected action.
