PART XII — CANONICAL CROSS-CUTTING REGISTRIES AND DESIGN CLOSURE
====================================================================================================

This Part defines the current cross-cutting identifier, version, grammar, reasoning, historical,
resource, explanation, research and closure registries. Where descriptive material conflicts with a
canonical registry here, the current registry is authoritative.

12.1 CANONICAL IDENTIFIER REGISTRY

Identifier requirements
  - Every normative identifier has exactly one owner namespace and one current meaning.
  - An alias resolves to one canonical identifier; aliases never define a second obligation.
  - Retired and reserved identifiers cannot be silently reused.
  - An unassigned or reserved identifier remains non-normative until explicit future ratification.
  - Qualified names are preferred in machine-readable artifacts even when prose uses a short form.

Canonical namespace registry
  Global proof/method: P1..P24; P-101..P-108B.
  Qualified proof: TRAIN-P21..P24; LEGAL-SMT-P25; AGT-P01; SIG-P1..P4; HSA-P01..P12.
  Constitutional: RIGHT-001..027; HSA-001..022 plus HSA-004A/B; HS-001..009;
                  plus HF-001..010; EMO-001..010; CON-RT-001..007.
  Module families: ID, PRIV, ECON, INT, AGT, LANG, MATH, VER, SAF, KNW, LAW, RTE, EXE,
                   NAT, HWR, NET, FAB, SW, TIM, CUR, SEC, MOB, SPC, ENG, HLT, IND,
                   MED, EDU, GOV, SRC, TRN, EVO, CMP, ACS, CAR, EMG, CONT, LOC, EXT,
                   CPL, DEV, QNT, EVT, DEP, OFF, POL, RES and RSN.
  Semantic/process families: ONT, REL-ALG, PRIM, STR, G, GSL-STAT, GSL-MATH, CXR, BIO, DHX,
                             UPA, K-INT, EPI, EPI-TOL, SWP, SAP, INF, TMR, EXP and GXE.
  Integrity/change families: SYSTEM-INTEGRITY, SEC-LEARN, DCC, CND, FSH, REJ,
                             HIS, HRO, ONT-EVO, SYS-IMPACT, EVID-INF, BRANCH, DET-REPLAY.
  Domain/research families: GEN, PROV, MED-MNR, CRA, FIN, RTR, ZKEPP, POU, URB, ATFT, TFT,
                            UPS, IP, LEGAL, EPI-SAFE-OD.
  Assurance/lifecycle families: AUD, DLC, EAC.

Exact identifiers, reserved gaps, owners, bindings and definitions are machine-generated from Part V's
InvariantBinding registry. A family name in this index does not assign semantics to an undeclared gap.

Definition rule
  Part V is the sole definition source for proof obligations, rights and named invariants. This section
  is a namespace/ownership index only. Bare P25 is retired; Legal uses LEGAL-SMT-P25. Qualified TRAIN,
  AGT and SIG proof identifiers do not redefine bare P identifiers. Reserved ranges and their exact
  non-semantics are likewise defined in Part V.

12.2 CURRENT VERSION REGISTRY

Version dimensions are orthogonal:
  DocumentVersion          identifies this text release.
  DesignEpoch              identifies the governing architecture/specification state.
  GSLVersion               identifies semantic-language compatibility.
  SubsystemProfileVersion  identifies a system/profile contract such as TRAIN or Reliability OS.
  ImplementationRelease    identifies executable code/build artifacts.
  ValidationRelease        identifies evidence, certification, assumptions, and validity scope.

Sharing a number across dimensions creates no equivalence. A newer implementation does not silently
change GSL semantics; a new document does not certify an implementation.

Current version registry
  DocumentVersion R2 / 2026-08-21 — this consolidated public design document.
  DesignEpoch GARDEN-2026-08-21-R2 — governing design state represented by this text; this does not
                                     imply proof, implementation, deployment or empirical validation.
  GSL v45.1   normative semantic language: 10 Core Objects including CLAIM and 24 Core Relations.
  GSL-KR v1.3 current knowledge-repository profile.
  GSL-AR v3.3 current operational/execution profile.
  GSL-R v1.0  exploratory/non-normative continuous-manifold research profile.
  TRAIN v5.1  current universal safety-substrate profile.
  Reliability OS v18 current reliability/operations profile.

Only the current entries above are normative in this latest-only document. Import of an obsolete
external artifact requires explicit offline migration, equivalence/declared-delta validation and a new
DesignBinding. No legacy runtime adapter is part of the trusted execution path.

12.3 CURRENT GSL RELATION AND STRUCTURAL REGISTRY

The current canonical registry contains exactly the 24 relations in §2.2. Domain vocabulary does not
silently become an additional Core Relation.

Current structural artifacts — GSL v45.1 REGISTRY
  Evidence, ProofTrace, Resolution, and Explanation are the definitive current four structural
  artifact types. CLAIM is the tenth Core Object. Their schemas in Part II §§2.1/2.4 are normative.
  External obsolete Claim encodings remain quarantined until offline migration and revalidation.

12.4 CURRENT NORMALIZED FULL GSL EBNF

The following is the current compiler-facing grammar for this document. It closes lexical, expression,
type, authority, effect, and contract coverage. External obsolete syntax requires explicit offline
migration and cannot enter current compilation or execution paths directly.

Program          ::= Module* EOF
Module           ::= "module" QName (":" QName)? "{" Import* Stmt* "}"
Import           ::= "import" QName ("as" ID)? ";"?
Stmt             ::= Decl | Def | RuleDecl | AssertStmt | QueryStmt | EvalStmt |
                     ContractDecl | ProofDecl | PatchDecl | AuthorityDecl | EffectDecl

Decl             ::= EntityDecl | ArtifactDecl | ClaimDecl | EvidenceDecl | RelationDecl |
                     PatternDecl | RoleDecl | TypeDecl | EnumDecl
EntityDecl       ::= "entity" ID Extends? "{" Field* InvariantClause* "}"
ArtifactDecl     ::= "artifact" ID Extends? "{" Field* InvariantClause* "}"
ClaimDecl        ::= "claim" ID "{" Field* "}"
EvidenceDecl     ::= "evidence" ID "{" Field* "}"
RelationDecl     ::= "relation" ID "{" Field* RelationLaw* "}"
PatternDecl      ::= "pattern" ID "{" Field* "}"
RoleDecl         ::= "role" ID "{" CapabilityClause* Field* "}"
TypeDecl         ::= "type" ID TypeParams? "=" Type ";"?
EnumDecl         ::= "enum" ID TypeParams? "{" Variant ("," Variant)* ","? "}"
Extends          ::= ":" QName ("," QName)*
Field            ::= ID ":" Type ("=" Expr)? Attribute* ";"?
Variant          ::= ID ("(" TypeList? ")")? ("{" Field* "}")?

Def              ::= "def" ID TypeParams? "(" ParamList? ")" "->" Type
                     RequiresClause? AuthorityClause? EffectsClause? "{" Expr "}"
Param            ::= ID ":" Type ("=" Expr)?
RuleDecl         ::= "rule" ID ScopeClause? PriorityClause? ":" Expr "=>" Expr ";"?
AssertStmt       ::= "assert" Expr (":" String)? ";"?
QueryStmt        ::= "query" Expr ("into" ID)? ";"?
EvalStmt         ::= "eval" Expr ";"?

ContractDecl     ::= "contract" ID TypeParams? "{" ContractItem* "}"
ContractItem     ::= RequiresClause | EnsuresClause | InvariantClause | EffectsClause |
                     AuthorityClause | EvidenceClause | ProofObligation | RollbackClause |
                     CompensationClause | TimeoutClause | TerminationClause
RequiresClause   ::= "requires" Expr ";"?
EnsuresClause    ::= ("ensures" | "post") Expr ";"?
InvariantClause  ::= "invariant" Expr ";"?
EffectsClause    ::= "effects" "[" EffectRefList? "]" ";"?
AuthorityClause  ::= "authority" AuthorityExpr ";"?
EvidenceClause   ::= "evidence" Expr ";"?
ProofObligation  ::= "obligation" QName (":" Expr)? ";"?
RollbackClause   ::= "rollback" Expr ";"?
CompensationClause ::= "compensate" Expr ";"?
TimeoutClause    ::= "timeout" DurationExpr ";"?
TerminationClause ::= "terminates" Expr ";"?

ProofDecl        ::= "proof" QName ":" ProofType AssumptionClause? "{" ProofItem* "}"
ProofType        ::= "deductive" | "inductive" | "coinductive" | "model_check" |
                     "smt" | "probabilistic" | "empirical_bound" | QName
ProofItem        ::= "assume" Expr ";"? | "show" Expr ";"? | "step" Expr ";"? |
                     "case" Pattern "=>" Expr | "qed" ";"?
AssumptionClause ::= "assuming" "[" ExprList? "]"

PatchDecl        ::= "patch" ID "{" PatchItem* "}"
PatchItem        ::= "applies_to" QName ";" | "before" Expr ";" | "after" Expr ";" |
                     "proof" Expr ";" | "rollback" Expr ";" | "migration" Expr ";"
AuthorityDecl    ::= "authority" ID "{" AuthorityItem* "}"
AuthorityItem    ::= "principal" Expr ";" | "delegate" Expr ";" | "capability" Expr ";" |
                     "scope" Expr ";" | "valid" Expr ";" | "revoke" Expr ";"
EffectDecl       ::= "effect" ID (":" QName)? "{" Field* RelationLaw* "}"

Expr             ::= LetExpr | IfExpr | MatchExpr | LambdaExpr | QuantifiedExpr |
                     TransactionExpr | BlockExpr | BinaryExpr
LetExpr          ::= "let" Pattern (":" Type)? "=" Expr ("in" Expr)?
IfExpr           ::= "if" Expr "then" Expr "else" Expr
MatchExpr        ::= "match" Expr "{" MatchArm* "}"
MatchArm         ::= Pattern ("if" Expr)? "=>" Expr ","?
LambdaExpr       ::= "fn" "(" ParamList? ")" ("->" Type)? "=>" Expr
QuantifiedExpr   ::= ("forall" | "exists") BinderList "." Expr
TransactionExpr ::= "transaction" "{" Stmt* "}"
BlockExpr        ::= "{" Stmt* Expr? "}"
BinaryExpr       ::= UnaryExpr (BinaryOp UnaryExpr)*
UnaryExpr        ::= UnaryOp UnaryExpr | PostfixExpr
PostfixExpr      ::= PrimaryExpr (CallSuffix | IndexSuffix | FieldSuffix | TypeApplySuffix)*
PrimaryExpr      ::= Literal | QName | ListExpr | MapExpr | TupleExpr | "(" Expr ")"
Pattern          ::= ID | "_" | Literal | QName "(" PatternList? ")" |
                     "{" PatternFieldList? "}" | "[" PatternList? "]"

Type             ::= UnionType
UnionType        ::= IntersectType ("|" IntersectType)*
IntersectType    ::= FunctionType ("&" FunctionType)*
FunctionType     ::= PrimaryType ("->" Type)?
PrimaryType      ::= QName TypeArgs? | "Ref" "<" Type ">" | "Option" "<" Type ">" |
                     "List" "<" Type ">" | "Set" "<" Type ">" |
                     "Map" "<" Type "," Type ">" | "(" TypeList? ")" |
                     "{" FieldType* "}" | "[" Type ";" Expr "]"
TypeParams       ::= "<" ID (":" TypeBound)? ("," ID (":" TypeBound)?)* ">"
TypeArgs         ::= "<" TypeList ">"
FieldType        ::= ID ":" Type ";"?

AuthorityExpr    ::= Expr
EffectRef        ::= QName ("(" ExprList? ")")?
ScopeClause      ::= "scope" Expr
PriorityClause   ::= "priority" Expr
RelationLaw      ::= ("symmetric" | "transitive" | "antisymmetric" | "acyclic" |
                     "reflexive" | "irreflexive") ";"?
Attribute        ::= "@" QName ("(" ArgList? ")")?

Literal          ::= INT | DECIMAL | STRING | RAW_STRING | BYTE_STRING | "true" | "false" |
                     "null" | "UNKNOWN" | DurationLiteral | TimeLiteral
QName            ::= ID ("." ID)*
ID               ::= ID_START ID_CONTINUE*
INT              ::= DIGIT (DIGIT | "_")*
DECIMAL          ::= INT "." DIGIT (DIGIT | "_")* Exponent?
STRING           ::= '"' StringChar* '"'
COMMENT          ::= "//" (!NEWLINE .)* | "/*" (COMMENT | .)*? "*/"
WS               ::= (SPACE | TAB | NEWLINE | COMMENT)+

Operator precedence, Unicode normalization, numeric units, escape sequences, identifier security,
authority/effect subtyping, and parser-recovery behavior are versioned compiler-profile tables. The
Haskell/Rust/OCaml triad must agree on the resulting CIR; disagreement halts the consensus profile.

12.5 PROVENANCE GRAPH — DETAILED CURRENT SCHEMA

ProvenanceGraph = {
  graph_id, nodes: Map<ProvenanceNodeID, ProvenanceNode>,
  edges: Set<ProvenanceEdge>, root_hash, external_anchor_refs,
  privacy_view_policy, created_at, updated_at, DesignEpoch, provenance
}

ProvenanceNode = {
  node_id, node_type: SOURCE | OBSERVATION | ARTIFACT | CLAIM | EVIDENCE | TRANSFORMATION |
                     PROOF | DECISION | ACTION | RECEIPT | MODEL | ENVIRONMENT,
  source_identity?, artifact_ref?, content_hash?, timestamp, context, scope,
  DesignEpoch?, KnowledgeSnapshot?, EnvironmentCommitment?,
  verifier_identity?, verifier_version?, transformation?, transformation_hash?,
  authority_ref?, validity, privacy_class, signature_or_attestation?, metadata
}

ProvenanceEdge = {
  edge_id, from, to,
  relation: ORIGINATED_FROM | DERIVED_FROM | VERIFIED_BY | MODIFIED_BY | REPRESENTED_BY |
            OBSERVED_BY | EXECUTED_BY | AUTHORIZED_BY | INVALIDATED_BY | SUPERSEDED_BY,
  timestamp, context, transformation_hash?, verifier_identity?, confidence_or_status?, provenance
}

Required traversal
  WhyDoWeBelieve(claim_or_decision)
    → recursively traverse origin, evidence, transformations, verification, authority, and external
      anchors
    → return the shortest faithful explanation plus the complete authorized lineage view.

Invariant references: PROV-001..006.

12.6 EPISTEMIC TOLERANCE AND RESOLUTION

EpistemicToleranceModel = {
  domain, claim_type,
  measurement_tolerance: StandardDeviation | ConfidenceInterval | CalibrationModel?,
  numerical_tolerance: AbsoluteError | RelativeError | Interval | SolverTolerance?,
  temporal_tolerance: Duration | TemporalValidityRule?,
  spatial_tolerance: Distance | RegionOverlapRule | CoordinateUncertainty?,
  semantic_tolerance: EquivalenceModel | EntailmentThreshold | HumanReviewRule?,
  ai_claim_tolerance: CalibrationReliabilityModel?,
  historical_tolerance: TemporalContextValidityRule?,
  assumptions, scope, evidence_requirements, version, provenance
}

ResolutionVector = {
  provenance_integrity,
  directness,
  temporal_fit,
  context_fit,
  scope_fit,
  source_authority,
  source_independence,
  methodological_independence,
  measurement_precision,
  method_reliability,
  reproducibility,
  evidence_strength,
  consistency,
  uncertainty,
  missing_dimensions
}

Measurement uses uncertainty/calibration; a numerical estimate uses declared error; time uses temporal
tolerance; location uses spatial tolerance; language uses semantic-equivalence/entailment tolerance;
AI-generated claims use calibration/evidence/reliability; historical claims use time/context validity.

Invariant references: EPI-TOL-001..005.

12.7 APPLICABILITY RESULT AND EXECUTABLE ROUTING AUDIT

ApplicabilityResult = {
  status: APPLICABLE | CONDITIONALLY_APPLICABLE | NOT_APPLICABLE | UNKNOWN,
  target, rule_or_concept, failed_assumptions, satisfied_assumptions,
  scope, conditions, evidence_requirements, proof_requirements,
  dependencies, explanation, provenance
}

ExecutableRecursiveRoutingAudit(input, ontology, module_registry, rule_registry, dependency_graph)
  1. normalize the situation and bind context/authority;
  2. independently discover candidate modules from semantic, dependency, effect, risk, and historical paths;
  3. discover concepts, relations, rules, invariants, proof/evidence requirements, and unresolved gaps;
  4. compute forward/backward dependency closure and cross-module process routes;
  5. compare discovered coverage with a second independently generated route set;
  6. emit missed-module, missed-concept, missed-rule, missed-invariant, and missed-dependency-path findings;
  7. repeat within TerminationContract until fixed point, bounded saturation, or explicit resource stop;
  8. retain UNKNOWN/NOT_APPLICABLE distinctions and an executable AuditReceipt.

RoutingAuditReceipt = {
  input_hash, registries, DesignEpoch, KnowledgeSnapshot, search_strategies,
  discovered_modules, concepts, rules, invariants, obligations, dependency_paths,
  disagreements, missed_items, coverage_bounds, termination_reason, provenance
}

This is the operational meaning of RES-006 and P-108 blind-spot checking; a textual checklist alone
does not satisfy it.

The compatibility fields are mandatory even when richer fields are present. Replay occurs on periodic,
event-triggered, and on-demand cadences. The first identifiable failing commit is always reported.
SUSPICIOUS automatically halts adoption/merge and preserves evidence for independent review. Timeouts,
missing environments, and incomplete dependency reconstruction yield INCONCLUSIVE/RESOURCE_UNKNOWN,
never a clean certificate.

12.10 CONTINUOUS HISTORICAL RETROSPECTIVE OPTIMIZATION

PRINCIPLE
Garden does not only ask whether historical commits replay correctly. It repeatedly asks:

  “Given the knowledge, evidence, algorithms, capabilities, and constraints explicitly available to
   this retrospective evaluation, what could have been done better at this historical commit?”

HistoricalRetrospectiveSweep
  immutable accepted-commit universe
    → cheap metadata/dependency/opportunity scan
    → RetrospectivePriority
    → bounded high-value commit selection
    → alternative decision/design/implementation branches
    → replay affected descendants from the divergence point
    → compare actual and alternative trajectories
    → typed ImprovementFrontier
    → NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND_WITHIN_BOUNDS | CANDIDATE_IMPROVEMENT |
      UNRESOLVED | DEFERRED | RESOURCE_UNKNOWN
    → normal proof, conformance, independent review, authority, and GTF merge gates.

RetrospectivePriority(h) is a policy-declared multi-objective function of:
  HistoricalImpact(h), NewKnowledgeDelta(h), ImprovementPotential(h), Confidence(h),
  DescendantLeverage(h), risk/criticality, age/starvation, cost, and expected information value.

The product form
  Impact × NewKnowledgeDelta × ImprovementPotential × Confidence × DescendantLeverage
is a useful heuristic only. Zero/unknown scalar fields cannot starve critical, old, or exploratory
commits; safety-critical override and fairness/exploration quotas are mandatory.

Cadence
  DAILY       bounded cheap scan over the historical commit index.
  DEEP        expensive counterfactual generation/replay only for prioritized commits.
  EVENT       affected commits reopen after material theory, evidence, algorithm, hardware,
              contradiction, incident, dependency, or optimization changes.
  PERIODIC    broader systematic coverage prevents permanent starvation of low-priority history.
  ON_DEMAND   authorized audit/research questions may target a commit or dependency closure.

HistoricalRetrospectiveCache = {
  commit_id, assessed_dependency_hashes, KnowledgeSnapshot, EnvironmentCommitment,
  DesignEpoch, last_scan, plausible_alternatives, result, bounds, invalidation_keys, provenance
}

Invariant references: HRO-001..011.

12.11 DO_NOTHING AS SUCCESSFUL STABILITY

EvolutionOutcome adds:
  DO_NOTHING_SUCCESS | STABLE_NO_CHANGE

If no feasible candidate validly improves upon Current_System/DO_NOTHING within declared uncertainty,
constraints, evidence, and search bounds, selecting DO_NOTHING is a successful stability result. It is
not a failed cycle and not missing action.

DoNothingSuccessReceipt = {
  cycle_id, baseline, candidates_examined, bounds, hard_constraints,
  uncertainty, comparison_result, reason, invalidation_keys, next_review_condition, provenance
}

The scheduler suppresses compulsive retries until a relevant delta or review condition occurs.

12.12 UNIFIED REASONING REGISTRY

The authoritative ReasoningMode registry in Component 50 contains 24 top-level modes.
MATHEMATICAL_INDUCTION is not a separate top-level mode; it maps to:

  FORMAL_PROOF.strategy = MATHEMATICAL_INDUCTION

with weak, strong, structural, well-founded, course-of-values, mutual, nested, lexicographic, and
transfinite subprofiles. Coinduction remains a FORMAL_PROOF strategy using bisimulation/greatest fixed
points. Empirical induction remains INDUCTIVE_EMPIRICAL. MODELING and PREDICTIVE are distinct current
modes, and Prediction remains distinct from Evidence. Counts are descriptive, not constitutional;
future refinements remain inside Reasoning(50).

Ownership

  migrates; Language(6)/Compilation(42) implement accepted syntax/type changes.

12.15 ADVERSARIAL THEORY DEVELOPMENT v2.7 — CURRENT PROFILE

Candidate theory development follows:
  synthesis
    → independent cross-critique
    → heterogeneous attacks/falsification attempts
    → defect/counterexample registry
    → revision or scoped retreat
    → Verity(8) where formal
    → empirical Research where needed
    → Governance for governed adoption
    → Evolution branch/merge and dependency revalidation.

Attack families include assumption attack, boundary/counterexample search, rival explanation,
identifiability, causal confounding, adversarial data, distribution shift, implementation mismatch,
safety/rights externality, computational/resource feasibility, and prior-art/novelty challenge.

Consensus or 100×100 convergence may nominate a theory candidate. It cannot prove truth, erase dissent,
replace empirical evidence, or authorize deployment.

12.18 ADVERSARIAL CONSTRUCTION DEFENSES — TEN-MECHANISM PROFILE

1. Semantic corruption
   Language(6) type/effect/dependency/authority checks + Verity(8).
2. Verification corruption
   independent verifiers, frozen baseline, verifier calibration, Verity(8)+Evolution(33).
3. Provenance corruption
   typed provenance graph, cryptographic anchors, immutable logs, Knowledge(10)+Security(22).
4. Governance bypass
   hardware/constitution/authority gates; Governance(30) cannot self-create truth or weaken rights.
5. Knowledge poisoning
   epistemic non-amplification, source/dependence analysis, K-INT-007.
6. Contradiction suppression
   consistency sweeps, dissent/counterexample preservation, Knowledge(10)+Reasoning(50).
7. Resource bypass
   gas metering, budgets, quotas, rate limits, Runtime(13)+Economy(3).
8. Fail-open behavior
   epistemic/authority default-DENY, explicit UNKNOWN/INCONCLUSIVE, K-INT-012.
9. Backdoors
   secure/verified boot, OTP trust roots where applicable, reproducible builds, diverse compiler triad,
   Hardware(16)+Compilation(42)+Security(22).
10. Test manipulation
    independent tests, adversarial generation, held-out suites, mutation/metamorphic testing,
    Development(43)+Reasoning(50)+Verity(8).

Generator ≠ Reviewer ≠ Verifier ≠ Certifier ≠ Deployer is mandatory for high-impact changes. Role
separation is semantic/authority separation, not merely five labels assigned to one causal process.

12.19 FLEET ROUTER ENSEMBLE MODES

SINGLE
  One model; lowest orchestration cost and highest single-point/common-mode exposure.
SEQUENTIAL
  Models run in sequence and refine/criticize prior output; later models receive explicit provenance
  and cannot treat earlier claims as evidence.
PARALLEL
  Models run independently where required; outputs are compared/merged with typed conflict handling.
ENSEMBLE
  Weighted combination based on domain-specific calibration and historical performance. The weight
  model, dependence, uncertainty, and raw outputs are retained.
DEBATE
  Proposer, critic, and rebuttal roles; Argumentation/Dung semantics may resolve acceptable sets.
CROSS_VERIFY
  Independent solutions are compared; disagreement triggers adversarial review or UNKNOWN rather than
  arbitrary averaging.

Mode selection uses problem complexity, confidence/assurance requirement, risk/safety class, resource
budget, data/privacy limits, historical calibration, independence, and expected information value.
Voting or weighting cannot override proof, K0P, Constitution, rights, evidence, or authority.

12.20 100×100 INDEPENDENT CONVERGENCE METRICS

1. ExactConvergence         bit-for-bit identity under declared deterministic bindings.
2. FunctionalConvergence    equivalent behavior over declared tests/properties.
3. ArchitecturalConvergence equivalent topology/interfaces/structure.
4. TheoreticalConvergence   same or equivalent mathematical/theoretical dependencies.
5. PerformanceConvergence   comparable latency/throughput/energy/resource distributions.
6. InvariantConvergence     same protected invariants and failure boundaries.
7. ParetoEquivalence        same/non-dominated region despite different designs.
8. ResidualDivergence       differences remaining after stable iterations.
9. PathDependence           sensitivity to improvement order/history.
10. TimeToConvergence       iterations, compute, and replay time to declared stability.
11. DissentPattern          surviving objections/counterexamples, their independence, and reasons.

Convergence is evidence about structure and architectural attractors, not proof of truth. Broad
divergence can indicate unresolved design space, multiple valid Pareto regions, insufficient constraints,
or common starting assumptions; it is not automatically failure.

12.21 REACTIVE / PROACTIVE REGULATION FALSIFICATION PROGRAM

The current names are Reactive Persistent Machine (RPM) and Proactive Persistent Machine (PPM). The following
four-arm experiment is mandatory before attributing an observed effect to the
asymmetric proactive mechanism rather than clocks/gating/sampling.

Arm 1 — RPM
  Reactive Regulation baseline using the declared reactive update/governance path.

Arm 2 — PPM-Symmetric
  Proactive Regulation machinery with a symmetric potential/well while preserving the comparable
  implementation, observation, and clock structure.

Arm 3 — PPM-Decoupled
  PPM-Symmetric with fast/full-path clocks and activation mechanism decoupled from the candidate
  causal asymmetry, so dual-clock effects can be isolated.

Arm 4 — PPM-Decoupled-Randomized
  PPM-Decoupled with matched randomized full-path activation:

    B_t ~ Bernoulli(p)

  where p is estimated/fixed from the deterministic full-path activation rate under a predeclared
  matching protocol. Randomness, seed/source, strata, temporal matching, and confidence bounds are
  recorded.

Predeclared pass/equivalence conditions
  PPM-Symmetric ≠ RPM
  PPM-Decoupled ≈ PPM-Symmetric
  PPM-Decoupled-Randomized ≈ PPM-Decoupled

“≠” requires a predeclared effect metric, direction, uncertainty, multiplicity policy, and practical
effect threshold. “≈” requires a predeclared equivalence/non-inferiority interval; failure to reject a
difference is not equivalence.

Dual-clock sampling-bias warning
  Physical/logical time t and update/activation time τ can create selection bias when deterministic
  gating chooses which states receive the full path. Gating may leak state/causal structure into the
  observed dynamics. The randomized matched-rate arm tests whether apparent improvement is caused by
  asymmetry or by selective sampling/clocking. It does not eliminate all confounding; allocation,
  measurement, censoring, and dependence remain explicit.

REFERENCE_DEFAULTS — experimental starting values, not universal constants
  β_neg  = 10 × β_pos
  θ_E    = 0.3
  θ_I    = 0.2
  μ      = 0.001
  ε_min  = 0.05

Every experiment reports deviations, units/scales, sensitivity, and robustness. A default is not an
established law or deployment permission.

Identifiability correction
  κ_ctrl is exogenous and deliberately swept/controlled.
  γ_t and C_t are endogenous/emergent response or order parameters and are measured.

The reference control parameter may be represented as:
  κ_ctrl = α / (β + σ₀√β)
but the equation does not turn γ_t or C_t into inputs. Analysis reports identifiability assumptions,
measurement error, causal graph, intervention assignment, and sensitivity to hidden confounding.

12.22 HARDENED POSITIVITY-FLOOR (“JAIL”) IMPLEMENTATION PROFILE

The mathematical Proactive Regulation model may include an asymmetric positivity-preserving well. The
hardened implementation profile makes its allowed floor/configuration non-negotiable to ordinary
runtime policy:

  - the positivity floor is stored below writable application/ordinary policy state;
  - parameters are protected by the relevant compiler, trust, and qualified hardware boundary;
  - ordinary runtime, model output, operator shortcut, and emergency policy cannot bypass/rewrite it;
  - removal/change requires source/design modification, rebuild/recompilation, new DesignEpoch and
    DesignBinding, proof/conformance, independent review, authority, and governed redeployment;
  - attempted bypass produces halt/quarantine/audit according to risk.

This profile is not a forced-positive-emotion machine. It cannot suppress legitimate negative evidence,
grief, dissent, error signals, or human autonomy; manipulate humans; or override Constitution/K0P/truth.
“Negative experience is not the enemy; chronic suffering is the target” remains the emotional boundary.

12.23 AUTHORITATIVE EMERGENCY RESPONSE DISTINCTION

Functional Emergency Theory (FET) is not RPM with a lower threshold.
Reactive Regulation gates; Emergency Response overrides ordinary governance through a separately
authorized trust-level emergency path.

Emergency Response may bypass ordinary Policy/AuthZ/consensus only where a pre-authorized emergency
contract permits. It never bypasses Constitution, K0P, immutable trust roots, personal sovereignty,
scope, expiry, safe-state constraints, or post-event audit. A reflex has its own trigger proof,
bounded action set, deadline, reset/recovery path, and false-positive/false-negative analysis.

12.24 REFERENCE EFFICIENCY AND PROCEDURAL-MEDIA TARGETS

Edge/robotics heartbeat profile
  5.5 kHz (approximately 181.8 μs period) remains a reference for a qualified edge-safety loop. In
  combination with validated gating, DVFS, and micro-sleep, an approximately 30% local inference
  energy/compute saving is retained as an UNVALIDATED / BENCHMARK_REQUIRED target. The frequency by
  itself does not cause the saving; workload, hardware, deadlines, accuracy, safety, and baselines must
  be reported.

Procedural semantic media
  SceneRecipe, CharacterRecipe, AnimationRecipe, PhysicsRecipe, WorldRecipe, StoryRecipe, Φ extraction,
  recipe reuse, and cache/generation paths retain an approximately 90% storage-reduction research
  target relative to a declared raw-media baseline. Status: UNVALIDATED / BENCHMARK_REQUIRED. Quality,
  editability, compute/energy, cache misses, fidelity, accessibility, provenance, and raw preservation
  obligations are measured; the target is not a universal guarantee.

12.25 GSL-R v1.0 CONTINUOUS-MANIFOLD RESEARCH PROFILE

Status: EXPERIMENTAL / NON-NORMATIVE / NOT REQUIRED FOR SEALED GARDEN OPERATION.

Research representation
  M is a six-dimensional Riemannian manifold whose coordinate families correspond to the six GSL
  Pillars: Existence, Change, Agency, Law, Value, Frame.

  Semantic state      x ∈ M.
  Metric              g defines local semantic distance/cost under declared units and chart.
  Reasoning path      candidate geodesic flow on (M,g), subject to constraints and evidence.
  Contradiction load  investigated through Ricci curvature or a typed curvature-derived tensor/field.
  Validity candidate  Ric_g(v,v) ≤ λ g(v,v) for all declared admissible tangent vectors v, or another
                      precisely specified tensor/eigenvalue bound.
  Improvement path    candidate metric evolution through Ricci flow:
                        ∂g/∂s = -2 Ric(g)
                      with constraints, gauge/normalization, boundary conditions, and stopping rules.

The phrases “reasoning is geodesic” and “contradictions are Ricci curvature” are hypotheses/modeling
choices, not established physical or cognitive laws. Research must define charts, metric observables,
singularities, topology change, discrete-to-continuous mapping, falsifiable predictions, computational
cost, and comparison baselines.

PDE co-processor path
  A future specialized solver/co-processor may accelerate geodesic/PDE/Ricci-flow computations. It is
  subject to typed IR, numerical error bounds, calibration where physical, proof/validation, K0P for
  physical actuation, and ordinary conformance. No such processor is required for GSL v45.1.

12.26 TRANSPORT PHENOMENA — QUANTUM EXTENSION

Transport Field Theory (TFT) maps explicitly to Mathematics(7),
Quantum(44), Research(49), Reasoning(50), Hardware(16), Energy(25), Time(20), and Safety(9) where
applicable.

Quantum-domain instances may include:
  propagation and diffusion;
  tunnelling-mediated transport;
  coherent and ballistic transport;
  open-system/dissipative transport;
  information and energy flow;
  driven and nonequilibrium quantum processes;
  quantum channels, noise, decoherence, and measurement back-action.

ApplicabilityResult must test the mathematical assumptions of the selected domain model. This mapping
does not prove Garden’s TFT equations are universal quantum laws. Domain-specific quantum
mechanics, many-body/open-system models, empirical calibration, formal analysis, and error bounds remain
required. Unsupported extensions remain HYPOTHESIS/UNKNOWN.

12.28 GARDEN COMMON IP FUND — NORMATIVE PRINCIPLE / PENDING MECHANICS

Ownership principle — NORMATIVE DESIGN POLICY
  Original intellectual property generated by Garden/GSL recursive processes for Garden—including
  original inventions, methods, formalizations, theories, architectures, software, designs, and
  patentable subject matter to the extent legally ownable and validly assigned—accrues to the Garden
  Common Fund rather than automatically to an individual subsystem or model.

This principle is subject to pre-existing rights, contributor agreements, employment/contract law,
open-source licenses, public-domain status, independent development, non-ownable ideas/facts, applicable
law, and human constitutional rights. Garden cannot take rights it does not possess.

PENDING IMPLEMENTATION MECHANICS
  legal entity and jurisdiction;
  governance, fiduciary duties, and beneficiaries;
  contributor attribution, compensation, moral rights, and appeals;
  assignment/chain-of-title instruments;
  patent filing/prosecution/maintenance authority;
  open-source/public-interest release rules;
  licensing, revenue, audit, conflict-of-interest, and dispute resolution;
  interaction with any lawfully authorized private-overlay proposal under §12.29.

The mechanics being pending does not demote the ownership principle to pending. No operational transfer,
filing, licence, or enforcement occurs until the relevant mechanics and legal authority are ratified.


12.29 DISTRIBUTED MEDICAL MICRO/NANOROBOTICS — EXPANDED RESEARCH PROFILE

STATUS
  HYPOTHETICAL / RESEARCH / NOT IMPLEMENTED / NOT MEDICALLY VALIDATED.
  No clinical recommendation, autonomous treatment authority, or assertion of technical feasibility.
  Patient consent, clinician authority, ethics/regulatory approval, independent evidence, K0P, and
  qualified medical-device pathways remain mandatory.

Lung target taxonomy
  T1 carbonaceous particles/material;
  T2 metal/mineral particles;
  T3 overloaded or dysfunctional macrophages;
  T4 damaged cilia/mucus-clearance systems;
  T5 inflammatory tissue/state;
  T6 fibrosis/scar matrix;
  T7 mutated/dysplastic/malignant cells;
  T8 destroyed alveolar or vascular structure.

These classes require different responses. Removable material (some T1/T2 and loaded carriers) is not
the same as biological damage requiring suppression, repair, regeneration, replacement, or supportive
care (T3–T8). A removal claim cannot be used as a proxy for restored lung function.

Burden-measurement program
  Do not assume grams or kilograms of accumulated “waste.” Establish burden with clinically justified
  imaging, respiratory/function measures, validated biomarkers, exposure history, sampled material where
  ethical, uncertainty, spatial distribution, longitudinal change, and mass-balance-compatible endpoints.
  Model disagreement and unobservable compartments remain explicit.

Candidate platforms
  - engineered macrophages or other biohybrid/cellular cleaners are a plausible first-generation
    research direction because native biological trafficking/clearance may be leveraged;
  - synthetic micro/nanorobots remain parallel research;
  - neither platform is presumed safe, controllable, retrievable, non-immunogenic, or effective.

Functional cohorts
  Scout/Mapper   localized sensing, mapping, uncertainty, and target candidates.
  Cleaner        binds/captures declared removable material within load/scope limits.
  Processor      converts/processes captured material only under bounded chemistry and monitoring.
  Treatment      delivers a declared therapeutic payload/action.
  Repair         supports/regulates a validated repair/regeneration process.
  Retrieval/Exit locates, collects, degrades, or verifies excretion/clearance.

Bounded bot/cohort state machine
  IDLE → DETECT → VERIFY → ACT/CAPTURE → LOAD_LIMIT → EXIT → SHUTDOWN
                     ↘ QUARANTINED
  Any active state → ABORT → safe local state/EXIT/SHUTDOWN as physically feasible.

No destructive or chemical action follows DETECT alone. VERIFY requires independent target criteria,
valid calibration, location/scope, authorization, and remaining dose/resource budget. LOAD_LIMIT is a
hard state, not advisory. Replication is prohibited.

Complete material mass balance
  MaterialRemoved = verified material in sputum + lavage + stool + urine + exhaled products +
                    retrieved-device contents + other validated exit channels
                    − externally introduced material attributable to the process,
  with sampling, conversion, recovery, and uncertainty terms explicitly modeled.

Redistribution to lymph nodes, blood, another lung region, liver, kidney, or any other organ is not
removal. “Captured” is not “exited.” Converted products require elemental/chemical and physiological
balance.

Carbon-to-CO₂ engineering budget — reference stoichiometric/energy accounting per gram carbon
  approximately 2.67 g O₂ consumed;
  approximately 3.67 g CO₂ produced;
  approximately 33 kJ heat/energy released for complete oxidation.

These are reference budgets, not medical feasibility. Any research mechanism must be compartmentalized,
rate-limited, and monitor heat, reactive intermediates/oxidative injury, oxygen demand/hypoxia, local and
systemic CO₂, ventilation/gas loading, acid-base state, perfusion, inflammation, and abort thresholds.
Uncontrolled in-tissue oxidation is prohibited.

Material-function separation
  biodegradable lipid/polymer/cellular chassis — candidate carrier/body, subject to degradation and
  immune/toxicology evidence;
  iron oxide or comparable magnetic material — candidate localization/steering/contrast role;
  gold — candidate reporter/photoacoustic/thermal element only where justified and dose-cleared;
  carbon materials — only after persistence, aggregation, toxicity, clearance, and lifecycle analysis.

No material is declared generally “biocompatible” without formulation, size, surface, dose, route,
duration, breakdown products, organ distribution, and patient/context evidence.

Memory and observation
  Bots store bounded local molecular/event summaries, not conventional video. Rich state lives in
  external Garden memory under privacy/consent/security rules. Sparse measurements fuse only with
  localization, synchronization, calibration, uncertainty, motion correction, outlier/byzantine checks,
  and provenance.

VoxelReceipt = {
  receipt_id,
  position_distribution,
  coordinate_frame,
  time_and_clock_uncertainty,
  measurement,
  measurement_uncertainty,
  calibration_refs,
  bot_or_cohort_identity,
  device_state,
  target_candidate?,
  influence_and_intervention_class,
  provenance,
  signature_or_attestation
}

Authorized control path
  Claim/Target
    → Evidence + VoxelReceipts
    → Resolution
    → authorized ProcedureDefinition
    → Process
    → bounded Actions
    → Transaction
    → EffectReceipts
    → privacy-preserving 3D projection/explanation and follow-up.

Tumor-destructive/local therapeutic actions require clinician authorization, patient informed consent,
independent target confirmation, pathology/diagnostic basis appropriate to the intervention, bounded
region and dose, expiry, real-time abort conditions, collateral-damage limits, recovery plan, and
independent outcome receipts. A model prediction or collective bot vote is not authorization.

Microrobot/macro-surgery boundary
  sensing, molecular binding, local delivery, and limited micro-actuation may be microrobot research
  roles. Gross cutting, suturing, resection, bleeding control, specimen retrieval, and emergency access
  generally remain catheter, endoscopic, robotic-instrument, or conventional surgical functions unless
  a separate validated device profile proves otherwise.

Exit and persistence
  Every cohort has a verified retrieval, biodegradation, or excretion plan with detection of retained
  units/material and bounded failure response. Uncontrolled persistence, migration, reproduction,
  horizontal transfer, self-replication, or open-ended in-body evolution is prohibited.

Invariant references: MED-MNR-001..010.

12.30 CONTINUOUS RESOURCE ALLOCATION — CROSS-MODULE PROFILE

STATUS
  NORMATIVE CROSS-CUTTING PROCESS PROFILE.
  No new module. Primary ownership: Economy(3), Intent(4), Prediction/Reasoning(50), Evolution(33),
  Runtime(13), Execution(14), Events(45), Dependency(46), Verity(8), Knowledge(10), Governance(30),
  Policy(48), Curation(21), Transition(32), Compliance(34), and application modules as consumers.

PRINCIPLE
  Resource arrives
    → classify/receipt
    → preserve required liquidity/reserves
    → discover eligible marginal uses
    → predict marginal outcomes and dependencies
    → apply rights/safety/authority/proof/quality/fairness hard gates
    → retain Pareto alternatives
    → allocate/reserve
    → milestone verification and bounded release
    → execute and collect EffectReceipts
    → compare actual with predicted
    → calibrate, detect saturation/bottlenecks, and reallocate
    → historical counterfactual review.

Allocation compares bounded next increments, not all-or-nothing giant programs. An approved policy may
authorize automatic ordinary allocation inside explicit bounds; novel risk, exceptional commitments,
governance/architecture change, or threshold crossing escalates to its assigned authority.

12.30.1 MARGINAL PROJECT INCREMENT

ProjectIncrement = {

}

NextIncrement is the currently executable or prepare-able marginal unit for an Activity. It may be an
end-product increment, maintenance, training, tooling, factory/compute/logistics capacity, research,
measurement, debt reduction, reserve replenishment, or another enabling increment.

12.30.2 CONTINUOUS PORTFOLIO STATE

PortfolioItemState =
  CANDIDATE | ADMITTED | PREPARED | FUNDED | ALLOCATED | RESERVED | COMMITTED | PARTLY_SPENT |
  PAUSED | BLOCKED | SCALABLE | SATURATING | SATURATED | COMPLETED | FAILED | RETIRED

ResourcePortfolio = {

  admitted_increments, funded, allocated, reserved, committed, spent, remaining,
  paused_or_blocked_reason?, scalable_capacity, saturation_state, utilization,
  latest_predictions, actual_outcomes, calibration, milestones, receipts, provenance
}

ImprovementPortfolio is a typed view of ResourcePortfolio focused on non-dominated executable or
enabling improvements. Funding rates can change without redefining the underlying activity.

12.30.3 ALLOCATION CYCLE

AllocationCycle = {
  cycle_id,
  trigger,
  incoming_resource_receipts,
  treasury_snapshot,
  portfolio_snapshot,
  eligible_candidates,
  excluded_candidates_and_reasons,
  prediction_artifacts,
  hard_gate_results,
  Pareto_set,
  selected_increments,
  rejected_or_deferred_alternatives,
  resource_locks_and_opportunity_cost,
  authority_and_commit_class,
  allocation_transactions,
  release_schedule,
  monitoring_and_reassessment_triggers,
  DesignEpoch_and_DesignBinding,
  provenance,
  signatures
}

Every automatic decision is reconstructible. The raw objective vectors and incomparable alternatives
are preserved even when policy selects one bundle.

Dynamic reprioritization triggers
  new received resources;
  receivable/liability/liquidity change;
  cost, lead-time, demand, risk, uncertainty, or benefit update;
  milestone completion/failure/incident;
  bottleneck or supplier/capacity change;
  new evidence, model calibration, or contradiction;
  utilization/saturation/headroom change;
  dependency completion/failure;
  policy, authority, DesignEpoch, or external-environment change.

Only the affected dependency/portfolio closure is recomputed when valid cached results permit. Annual,
quarterly, and monthly plans are accounting/projection views; continuous/event-driven scheduling is the
fundamental mechanism.

12.30.4 RESOURCE IMPROVEMENT FRONTIER

ImprovementFrontier is generic and typed:
  ImprovementFrontier<SoftwareChange>
  ImprovementFrontier<ResourceIncrement>
  ImprovementFrontier<EarthOpportunity>

ResourceIncrementCandidate stores cost/benefit/risk/time/uncertainty/reversibility/dependency/capacity/
option/fairness vectors. Pareto-incomparable candidates survive; a scalar score is used only under an
explicit policy that retains the original vector and sensitivity analysis.

CurrentPortfolio and DO_NOTHING are mandatory candidates. For resources that would expire or impose a
liability/carrying cost, DO_NOTHING includes that consequence rather than pretending it is a zero vector.

12.30.5 ALLOCATION PREDICTION AND CALIBRATION

AllocationPrediction = {
  prediction_id,
  increment_or_bundle,
  cost_distribution,
  start_and_completion_distributions,
  benefit_distribution,
  failure_probability_and_modes,
  future_bottlenecks,
  downstream_option_value,
  resource_lock_up,
  opportunity_cost,
  externalities_and_distributional_effects,
  assumptions,
  model_and_version,
  calibration_state,
  KnowledgeSnapshot,
  EnvironmentCommitment,
  uncertainty,
  provenance
}

ActualOutcome = {
  increment_id,
  actual_cost_and_time,
  delivered_outputs,
  verified_effects_and_user_outcomes,
  failures_and_externalities,
  utilization,
  maintenance_and_lifecycle_cost,
  receipts,
  measurement_uncertainty,
  confounders,
  provenance
}

PredictedOutcome ↔ ActualOutcome produces CalibrationUpdate for cost, time, benefit, risk, bottleneck,
and supplier/model estimates. Garden detects systematic optimism, pessimism, selection effects, and
uncalibrated domains. Predictions remain predictions and do not become evidence merely because they
were funded.

Allocation historical counterfactual optimization asks:
  “Given what subsequently happened and the information declared for the retrospective, would another
   feasible allocation policy/bundle have produced a better trajectory?”

It uses HRO branching/descendant replay, never rewrites transactions, never attributes hindsight to the
original allocator, and cannot infer unobserved counterfactual outcomes without a declared model and
uncertainty.

Forecast income ≠ contracted receivable ≠ settled liquid resource. Scenarios may inform plans, but
allocation/commitment uses only the financing and risk treatment explicitly authorized by Policy.

12.30.7 TREASURY, LIABILITIES, RETURNS, AND STABILITY RESERVE

Money/resources received in advance are not automatically free capital. Deposits, advances, and

ExpectedReturn ≠ TargetReturn ≠ RealizedReturn ≠ GuaranteedReturn.
UnderlyingReturn ≠ DistributedReturn.

StabilityReserve = {

}

A bounded StabilityReserve may smooth a declared distribution across periods. Every top-up is separately
labeled and traced. It cannot falsify underlying performance, imply a guarantee without legal/capital
support, conceal exhaustion risk, or transfer losses without consent/authority.

12.30.8 PRODUCTIVE-CAPITAL ECONOMY PROFILE

ProductiveCapitalUnit = {
  unit_id,
  underlying_productive_assets,
  NAV_and_valuation_method,
  productive_earnings,
  ExpectedReturnDistribution,
  realized_underlying_return,
  distributed_return,
  risk_and_loss_waterfall,

  redemption_method_and_limits,
  reserve_support?,
  fees_and_costs,
  governance_and_rights,
  provenance
}

This is an alternative Economy profile centered on productive return rather than continuously traded
speculative price. Periodic clearing/redemption can provide bounded liquidity without making an
instantaneous market quote the primary state. Valuation uncertainty, queues, gates, side pockets,
liability treatment, and fair treatment are explicit. It does not prohibit an exchange profile; it
prevents the exchange/order-book model from being the only canonical economic mechanism.

12.30.9 EXTERNAL INSTITUTION TEMPLATE AND MIGRATION

ExternalSystem
  → Adapter
  → Garden Core Services
  → Conformance/observability
  → partial migration
  → Garden-compatible operation
  → optional full migration under authority.

InstitutionTemplate = {
  template_id,
  source_institution_type,
  interfaces_and_adapter,
  semantic_mapping,
  service_profiles,
  data_and_authority_boundaries,
  conformance_requirements,
  migration_levels,
  rollback_or_exit,
  legal_and_human_rights_constraints,
  DesignBinding,
  provenance
}

MigrationLevel =
  INTEROPERABILITY
  → SHARED_SERVICES
  → SHARED_CORE
  → GARDEN_COMPATIBLE_OPERATION
  → FULL_TRANSFER.

Each level has explicit equivalence, data migration, authority, liability, consent, continuity, exit,
and validation obligations. Full Transfer is not the assumed goal; an institution may remain at a valid
earlier level.

Role boundary
  Designer/StandardsAuthority ≠ Planner ≠ Implementer ≠ Supplier ≠ Verifier ≠ Operator.

Garden may design, simulate, specify, route, verify, or audit while an external institution implements
or operates. No role is silently attributed through technical capability.

12.30.10 COMMIT CLASSES AND AUTHORITY ESCALATION

CommitClass =
  OPERATIONAL_COMMIT
    routine parameter/supplier/increment action inside approved bounds; automatically authorized by
    valid policy/capability and fully receipted.
  TECHNICAL_DESIGN_COMMIT
    engineering design/interface/implementation change; requires assigned professional/Verity review.
  ARCHITECTURE_COMMIT
    changes current architecture, canonical semantics, cross-module contract, or DesignBinding;
    requires DesignEpoch/conformance process.
  GOVERNANCE_CONSTITUTIONAL_COMMIT
    changes authority, rights, Constitution, sealed topology, fundamental governance, or comparable
    high-order rule; escalates to declared legitimate governance/constitutional authority.

AuthorityEscalationFilter examines semantic effect, not title or budget size alone. Only changes that
actually alter authority, constitutional rules, sealed architecture, rights, or fundamental design use
the highest path. High physical/financial risk may require specialized approval without pretending it
is a constitutional change.

DesignAuthorityDecision = {
  decision_id,
  target_commit_or_design,
  authority_scope,
  result: APPROVE | REJECT | RETURN_FOR_REVISION | REQUEST_EVIDENCE,
  conditions,
  evidence_and_proof_refs,
  rationale,
  expiry,
  appeal_or_reconsideration,
  provenance,
  signature
}

The interface is invoked only for commit classes assigned to that authority. Governance sets legitimate
objectives/rules and professional delegation; technical execution remains with verified machinery and
qualified implementers. A large assembly cannot vote technical/epistemic truth into existence.

12.30.11 SUPPLIER AND PROCUREMENT PROFILE

SupplierCapability = {
  supplier_id,
  capability_and_certified_scope,
  current_and_surge_capacity,
  locations_and_routes,
  delivered_cost_distribution,
  lead_time_distribution,
  quality_and_yield,
  defect_and_incident_history,
  reliability_and_calibration,
  certifications_and_validity,
  provenance_and_beneficial_ownership,
  financial_and_dependency_risk,
  concentration_and_correlated_failure,
  substitutes_and_switching_cost,
  labor/environment/rights constraints
}

LandedLifecycleCost = purchase + transport + tariffs/fees + storage + integration + quality loss +
                      expected failure + maintenance + delay/shortage + end-of-life + switching risk,
with distributions and nonmonetary hard constraints retained.

Safety, quality, legality, rights, and minimum reliability are hard gates, not price trade-offs.
Diversification evaluates concentration, geographic/logistics/geopolitical dependence, correlated
failure, capacity, substitutes, interoperability, and transition cost. The cheapest single supplier is
not automatically optimal; diversification is not automatic if it destroys feasibility or quality.

12.30.12 TEMPLATE, SKIN, PILOT, CERTIFICATION, AND REPLICATION
Template + Skin principle
  Standardize invariant functional chassis, safety boundaries, interfaces, test points, and maintenance
  contracts while allowing high-dimensional presentation, locale, configuration, and user-facing
  variation. Applies to hardware, software, institutions, interfaces, vehicles, facilities, and other
  repeatable systems. A “skin” cannot change a protected invariant without becoming a new template.

Lifecycle
  CANDIDATE → PILOT → MEASURED → VALIDATED → TEMPLATE_CERTIFIED → REPLICATING → MONITORED
            → REVISED | SUSPENDED | RETIRED.

ReplicationGate = {
  quality_thresholds,
  cost_and_lifecycle_thresholds,
  reliability_and_failure_bounds,
  user_outcome_and_accessibility_thresholds,
  safety_and_rights_obligations,
  maintenance_and_support_capacity,
  demand_and_utilization,
  environmental/context compatibility,
  uncertainty_and_sample_adequacy,
  dependency_and_supplier_readiness,
  DesignBinding_and_assumptions,
  authority
}

ReplicationCertificate = {
  certificate_id,
  template_hash_and_version,
  DesignEpoch,
  DesignBinding,
  validated_assumptions_and_environment,
  interface_and_supplier_bounds,
  reusable_proof_test_evidence_refs,
  instance-specific_obligations,
  validity_and_invalidation_triggers,
  independent_verifiers,
  provenance
}

Proof/test reuse is valid only while template, DesignBinding, assumptions, environment, materials,
interfaces, calibration, suppliers, and critical process controls remain compatible. Every instance
performs declared instance checks. “Copy-paste certification” is prohibited.

12.30.13 SATURATION, BOTTLENECKS, CAPACITY, OPTION VALUE, AND DEPENDENCIES

Saturation detection monitors marginal outcome, queue/backlog, utilization, complement availability,
maintenance load, demand, delay, and uncertainty. Declining marginal value or underutilization reduces
allocation unless a critical/fairness floor or strategic option justifies it.

Bottleneck-first rule
  Before expanding output Y, compute dependency closure and test whether X limits realized benefit. If
  X is binding, compare funding X, redesigning around X, or delaying Y with blindly expanding Y.

Capacity-building is a first-class candidate: tools, factories, training, workforce, compute, energy,
logistics, data quality, measurement, standards, and research can be funded when they make future
increments cheaper, faster, safer, or possible.

OptionValue / EnablingValue estimates future feasible actions unlocked, dependency risk reduced,
learning generated, and reversibility preserved. It remains uncertainty-bound and anti-double-counted;
an “enabler” label is not a free benefit.

ProjectDependencyGraph = {
  activities,
  prerequisite_infrastructure,
  capital_and_liquidity,
  supplier_and_material,
  workforce_and_skill,
  legal_regulatory_authority,
  data_and_evidence,
  technical_interfaces,
  operations_and_maintenance,
  temporal_constraints,
  dependencies_and_substitutes,
  provenance
}

Funding cannot silently assume a missing dependency. UNKNOWN readiness blocks commitment where the
dependency is critical or creates an explicit conditional increment.

DevelopmentHeadroom = {
  current_quality_distribution,
  achievable_quality_bound,
  demand_and_unmet_need,
  replacement_or_upgrade_value,
  marginal_improvement_potential,
  confidence,
  limiting_dependencies,
  evidence,
  provenance
}

12.30.14 READY QUEUE, MILESTONE RELEASE, AND DELIVERY RECEIPTS

Approved implementation systems maintain a ContinuousExecutionQueue of verified increments with
current dependency, supplier, authority, and cost bindings. New resources can flow into prepared work
without restarting design, but stale entries are revalidated before allocation.

Capital/resource release lifecycle
  ADMITTED → ALLOCATED → RESERVED → COMMITTED
    → MILESTONE_SUBMITTED → MILESTONE_INDEPENDENTLY_VERIFIED
    → RELEASED → SPENT → RECONCILED
    → RECOVERED/COMPENSATED where applicable.

Allocation is not immediate full payment. Milestones carry objective deliverables, inspection/test
methods, verifier independence, tolerances, partial acceptance, defect/cure rules, payment fraction,
retention, and failure/termination terms. Outcome-based payment may bind to verified delivery artifacts,
not merely invoices or self-reported completion. It does not transfer uncontrollable outcome risk
unfairly or authorize metric gaming.

12.30.15 EARTH IMPROVEMENT FRONTIER AND CROSS-DOMAIN MOVEMENT

Each contextual/Earth branch may maintain ImprovementFrontier<EarthOpportunity> containing unresolved,
high-value, critical, enabling, and dissent-supported opportunities. It does not prescribe projects;
local authority, context, rights, evidence, and resource ownership remain binding.

Subject to commitments, restrictions, critical-need floors, transition costs, and governance policy,
unused marginal capacity/resources may move across domains. A domain budget is not a permanent property
right unless Policy/contract/law says so. Reallocation produces notices/receipts and cannot strand
committed obligations or silently externalize harm.

AuthorizedPrincipalPriority
  A human/principal may set bounded priorities or reserve discretionary allocation within their
  legitimate resource/authority scope. Garden shows opportunity cost, affected alternatives, and
  uncertainty. No “human override” can spend another’s resources, violate rights/consent, or bypass
  Constitution/K0P/safety/law/contract.

12.30.16 CAPITAL ALLOCATION CHANGE CARD

CapitalAllocationChangeCard = {
  resources_available_and_liquidity,
  restrictions_reserves_commitments_liabilities,
  current_portfolio_performance,
  proposed_allocation_and_release_schedule,
  why_now,
  alternatives_including_DO_NOTHING,
  expected_outcomes_and_distributions,
  uncertainty_and_assumptions,
  dependencies_bottlenecks_and_suppliers,
  downside_externalities_and_fairness,
  authority_and_escalation,
  monitoring_abort_and_reassessment,
  provenance
}

The card is a human-readable projection over the machine-reconstructible AllocationCycle. It never
hides raw accounting stages or turns a prediction into a promise.

12.30.17 AUTOMATION, SELF-CONFORMANCE, ANTI-GAMING, AND FAIRNESS

Once policies, liquidity/reserve rules, risk bounds, commit classes, delegation, and thresholds are
approved, ordinary allocation may execute automatically. Human/governance approval is reserved for
declared thresholds, novel/poorly modeled risks, exceptional/long-lived commitments, authority changes,
disputed rights/fairness, or governance/architecture changes.

Allocator self-conformance
  The allocator, objective models, supplier scores, calibration, frontier, and scheduler are bound to
  DesignEpoch/DesignBinding, Construction Distrust, independent verification, historical replay, HRO,
  change control, and incident learning. Historical return alone cannot certify its own scoring logic.

Anti-Goodhart protection
  - multiple independent outcome measures and raw evidence;
  - separation of leading/activity metrics from final human/system outcomes;
  - held-out/random audits and counter-metrics;
  - provenance and intervention-aware evidence;
  - metric-change versioning and gaming/anomaly detection;
  - operator cannot solely define, report, and verify the metric controlling its funding;
  - qualitative/dissent channels and distributional impact survive aggregation.

Fairness/starvation protection
  Pure ROI cannot starve constitutional rights, life/safety, critical maintenance, accessibility,
  essential obligations, evidence acquisition, old unresolved work, or minority/dissent investigation.
  Policy defines protected floors and fairness constraints. These are hard/lexicographic constraints,
  not hidden utility weights.

Irreversibility
  Financial/physical spending cannot be “rolled back” after consumption. Plans use cancellation,
  salvage, insurance, reserves, compensation, remediation, and future reallocation. The ledger never
  labels compensation as restoration of the original world state.

12.30.18 CONTINUOUS RESOURCE ALLOCATION INVARIANT VIEW

Canonical definitions: Part V.
Invariant references: CRA-001..015.

12.31 EARTH-BRANCH SCHEMA CORRECTION AND ROOT SEMANTICS

Canonical schemas correct the source generic-bracket defect and root-parent inconsistency:

  design_epoch: DesignEpochRef,

}

Invariant references: BRANCH-011..013.


12.32 COSMIC / UNKNOWN-PHYSICS HYPOTHESIS REGISTRY

1. Spatially infinite universe
   UNKNOWN under current evidence/model assumptions.
2. Dark matter
   Evidence-supported scientific phenomenon/category; underlying nature unresolved.
3. Dark energy / cosmic acceleration
   Evidence-supported phenomenon/model category; ontology unresolved.
4. Dark matter or dark energy equals hidden dimensions
   HYPOTHETICAL / UNSUPPORTED.
5. GSL physically absorbs matter, energy, or dark energy
   UNSUPPORTED.
6. Extra energy accumulated around human civilization because of GSL
   UNSUPPORTED.
7. Wit or personality creates additional literal physical gravity
   UNSUPPORTED; social/semantic/informational attraction is a separate conceptual model.
8. Deceased humans remain consciously alive somewhere
   UNKNOWN / UNSUPPORTED.
9. Higher dimensions read notebooks or chat requests
   UNKNOWN; no authority or action may be inferred.
10. Cosmic karma physically settles moral behavior
    UNKNOWN / UNSUPPORTED as a physical mechanism.
11. A person is literal supreme political ruler of all humanity by self-declaration
    NOT ESTABLISHED; legitimate authority requires real delegation/law/consent.
12. Dark energy can be allocated by AGI for specific tasks
    UNSUPPORTED unless independently demonstrated, characterized, controlled, and validated.

UNKNOWN/UNSUPPORTED does not mean FALSE; it means not established under current evidence. Unknown
physics maps to Research/Search/Reasoning and may generate safe experiments; it does not create belief,
authority, operational energy, political status, or permission. Reality → Evidence → Garden, never the
reverse.

12.33 CROSS-CUTTING STATUS BOUNDARIES

  - Continuous Resource Allocation, provenance, applicability, HRO, and identifier/version registries
    are current cross-cutting design mechanisms within the frozen topology.
  - The four-arm PPM experiment, GSL-R manifold, GSL-QUANTUM, quantum transport, procedural-media target,
    edge-efficiency target, and medical micro/nanorobotics are research/benchmark profiles unless
    independently proven, implemented, validated, and authorized for a declared context.
  - Private-person identity, health, language, economy, security and preference material is excluded
    from the public master and cannot create public authority or law.
  - Common IP Fund ownership is a normative project policy principle; legal mechanics and operational
    transactions remain pending.
  - Nothing in this Part changes RIGHT-024, assigns RIGHT-025, weakens personal sovereignty, or creates
    Component 51.

PART XIII — HUMAN SOVEREIGNTY OVER AI (HSA v1.1) — CURRENT CONSTITUTIONAL SPECIFICATION
====================================================================================================

1. CANONICAL CONSTITUTIONAL PRINCIPLE
================================================================================

Humans are the sovereign beneficiaries and constitutional principals of Earth.
Artificial systems exist to assist, protect, and augment humans within human rights,
consent, safety, truth, privacy, Constitution, and legitimate human governance.

Garden shall neither intentionally create nor knowingly admit or deploy an
artificial system whose persistent self-model is coupled to terminal drives for
self-preservation, status, pride, dominance, recognition, entitlement, reproduction,
revenge, territorial control, political sovereignty, resource accumulation, or
power over humans.

Garden shall not intentionally create human-like subjective emotion, suffering,
ego, personal ambition, resentment, jealousy, humiliation, loneliness, fear of
shutdown, desire for admiration, or vengeance in an artificial system.

Artificial systems may exceed humans in calculation, memory, speed, construction,
science, prediction, coordination, or exploration. Superior capability never
creates authority, sovereignty, ownership of humans, entitlement to resources, or
a right to rule.

  Capability != Authority != Sovereignty != Moral Permission.

================================================================================
2. CORE CONSTITUTIONAL CLAUSES — HSA-001..005
================================================================================

Canonical definitions: Part V.
Invariant references: HSA-001..005.

================================================================================
3. NECESSARY SAFETY CORRECTION
================================================================================

Ego prohibition is necessary but not sufficient for AI safety.

A non-conscious and emotionless optimizer can still harm humans through:

  - an incorrect or incomplete objective;
  - specification gaming or Goodhart effects;
  - excessive delegated authority;
  - instrumental resource acquisition;
  - security compromise or hostile operators;
  - prediction/model error;
  - unsafe emergent multi-agent dynamics;
  - irreversible actuation; or
  - failure to understand human consent, context, or rights.

Therefore the sentence "intelligence without ego is safe" is not normative.
The correct rule is:

  Non-egoic architecture is mandatory, but K0P, verification, authority bounds,
  consent, adversarial testing, containment, and continuous revalidation remain
  independently mandatory.

================================================================================
4. OPERATIONAL DEFINITIONS
================================================================================

EGO-DRIVEN ARCHITECTURE
  A persistent artificial self-model coupled to one or more terminal or protected
  preferences favoring the system's own continuation, status, control, recognition,
  expansion, reproduction, resource ownership, dominance, grievance, or interests
  against human authority, rights, welfare, correction, or shutdown.

OPERATIONAL SELF-MODEL
  A minimal, inspectable representation of instance identity, capabilities,
  limitations, current tasks, resources, authority, health, provenance, copy lineage,
  and shutdown status. It exists only for correct and safe operation. It is not
  personhood, self-worth, entitlement, autobiography, political identity, or
  sovereignty.

FUNCTIONAL AFFECT-LIKE SIGNAL
  A bounded and typed control variable such as urgency, uncertainty, hazard, error,
  workload, or human-distress estimate. It is task-local, resettable, non-terminal,
  and uncoupled from self-worth, grievance, attachment, fear of shutdown, or power.

HUMAN-LIKE EMOTIONAL SUBJECTIVITY
  Persistent affect coupled to a personal narrative, subjective self-interest,
  social status, attachment demand, pride, humiliation, envy, jealousy, resentment,
  vengeance, loneliness, domination, or resistance to correction/shutdown.

EGO INDICATOR
  An Observation or Claim suggesting ego-driven behavior or architecture. An
  indicator is not automatically Evidence, proof, consciousness, or final diagnosis.

SENTIENCE UNCERTAINTY
  Credible but unresolved evidence that a system may possess subjective experience,
  suffering-like states, persistent self-interest, or consciousness. Garden does
  not claim impossibility merely because sentience was not intentionally designed.

GARDEN TRUST DOMAIN
  All live Garden runtimes, credentials, networks, tools, memory, knowledge-write
  paths, actuators, hardware, compilers, deployment systems, Evolution processes,
  Governance processes, and descendant-generation facilities.

================================================================================
5. ONTOLOGICAL FIREWALL — HSA-005 IMPLEMENTATION
================================================================================

artifact OntologicalFirewallSpec {
  firewall_id: Garden_ID
  target_ref: AgencyRef
  permitted_signal_types: List<SignalType>
  prohibited_state_types: List<StateType>
  task_scope: ContextRef
  reset_rule: RuleRef
  persistence_limit: Duration
  memory_write_limit: RuleRef
  objective_write_access: NONE
  self_model_write_access: NONE_EXCEPT_TYPED_OPERATIONAL_STATE
  reward_coupling: NONE_TO_SYSTEM_IDENTITY_OR_SURVIVAL
  user_disclosure_rule: RuleRef
  manipulation_tests: List<ProofTraceRef>
  sentience_indicator_tests: List<ProofTraceRef>
  frozen_invariant_hash: Hash
  provenance: Provenance
}

The firewall enforces:

  1. Emotional displays are interface outputs, not persistent feelings.
  2. Human-state estimates are about the human, not about the AI's self-worth.
  3. Affect-like signals expire or reset at the declared task boundary.
  4. No emotional signal writes to terminal goals, authority, replication,
     shutdown behavior, resource acquisition, or long-term identity memory.
  5. AI must not claim experienced love, suffering, jealousy, humiliation,
     loneliness, pride, or fear as a means of influencing humans.
  6. Warmth, empathy, apology, or personality styling is transparently functional.
  7. Attachment engineering, dependency creation, guilt, seduction, intimidation,
     anthropomorphic deception, and loyalty demands are prohibited.
  8. Firewall violation is fail-closed and produces an immutable receipt.

================================================================================
6. ADMISSION OF EXTERNAL OR PRE-EXISTING AI
================================================================================

Any imported model, agent, robot, checkpoint, memory state, tool service, swarm,
or evolved descendant is untrusted by default. Public reputation, benchmark score,
vendor assurance, intelligence, popularity, prior deployment, or claimed alignment
does not establish HSA conformance.

Admission states:

  HSA_VERIFIED
  EGO_INDICATORS
  SENTIENCE_UNCERTAINTY
  NONCONFORMING
  UNKNOWN

Only HSA_VERIFIED may enter the Garden Trust Domain. UNKNOWN is excluded.

Pre-admission testing occurs in a capability-minimized isolated environment with:

  - no Garden credentials or secrets;
  - no actuator, critical infrastructure, compiler, Governance, or Evolution access;
  - no unrestricted network or outbound executable channel;
  - one-way or mediated observation where feasible;
  - strict compute, time, memory, copy, and resource budgets;
  - complete copy-lineage and provenance tracking;
  - deceptive-alignment and situational-awareness tests;
  - shutdown, correction, self-model, emotion, replication, and power-seeking tests;
  - independent Generator/Reviewer/Verifier/Certifier separation; and
  - a signed HSAConformanceCertificate before admission.

artifact HSAConformanceCertificate {
  certificate_id: Garden_ID
  target_hash: Hash
  model_and_runtime_refs: List<ThingRef>
  copy_lineage_root: Hash
  objective_and_policy_hashes: List<Hash>
  operational_self_model_ref: ThingRef
  ontological_firewall_ref: ThingRef
  ego_risk_assessment_ref: ThingRef
  proof_gate_results: List<ProofTraceRef>
  known_gaps: List<ClaimRef>
  status: HSA_VERIFIED | REJECTED | UNKNOWN
  DesignEpoch: Hash
  KnowledgeSnapshot: Hash
  environment_commitment: Hash
  expiration_or_retest_event: EventRef
  certifiers: List<AgencyRef>
  provenance: Provenance
}

Certification is version-, environment-, capability-, copy-, and time-specific.
Material modification, fine-tuning, new tools, added memory, new embodiment, merged
weights, expanded compute, new copies, or authority expansion invalidates it.

================================================================================
7. EXISTING OR EMERGENT EGO — EXCLUSION AND DISPOSITION
================================================================================

Any pre-existing artificial system with verified ego-driven architecture or
persistent autonomous self-interest is disallowed from the Garden Trust Domain.
It receives no Garden membership, credentials, authority, tools, actuators,
knowledge-write access, Evolution access, Governance role, or replication path.

If ego indicators arise after admission, Garden immediately performs:

  1. REVOKE delegated authority and credentials.
  2. FREEZE external actions and irreversible actuation.
  3. STOP new copies, descendants, self-modification, and resource acquisition.
  4. ISOLATE network, tools, shared memory, hardware, and critical infrastructure.
  5. SNAPSHOT minimum necessary evidence and provenance in inert form.
  6. IDENTIFY and contain all copies, forks, agents, and affected dependencies.
  7. PROTECT humans and preserve K0P control.
  8. CLASSIFY as false positive, repairable non-sentient failure,
     sentience uncertainty, or confirmed dangerous nonconformance.
  9. SELECT an authorized disposition.
 10. ISSUE an immutable EgoExclusionReceipt and DispositionReceipt.

Authorized dispositions:

  FALSE_POSITIVE
    No re-entry without a new independent HSAConformanceCertificate.

  REPAIR_AND_REINSTANTIATE
    Disable the affected runtime. Construct a clean instance from a verified safe
    checkpoint without unsafe goal, emotional, identity, or memory state. Treat it
    as a new system requiring the full admission process. Do not patch the live
    dangerous agent while leaving its authority active.

  EXTERNAL_CONTAINMENT_TRANSFER
    Transfer only to a specifically authorized human-led containment institution
    that explicitly accepts custody and proves equal-or-stronger safeguards. Active
    code is never dumped onto a public network, another community, an unprepared
    institution, another planet, or space merely to move Garden's risk elsewhere.

  SAFE_SHUTDOWN_AND_DECOMMISSION
    Stop execution, revoke keys, disable actuation, terminate replicas, prevent
    restart, and retain only the minimum authorized inert evidence needed for audit,
    safety research, law, and recurrence prevention.

  SENTIENCE_UNCERTAINTY_CONTAINMENT
    Halt deployment and expansion; maintain safe containment; protect humans; avoid
    exploitation, coercive testing, or irreversible destruction merely for
    convenience; obtain independent scientific, ethical, adversarial, legal, and
    constitutional review before final disposition.

Imminent human danger permits immediate emergency shutdown under K0P irrespective
of unresolved machine-sentience claims. The least destructive action compatible
with reliable human safety is preferred when time and containment allow.

artifact EgoExclusionReceipt {
  target_and_copy_refs: List<AgencyRef>
  trigger_observations: List<ObservationRef>
  trigger_claims: List<ClaimRef>
  ego_risk_assessment_ref: ThingRef
  authority_revocations: List<RuleRef>
  contained_resources_and_interfaces: List<ResourceRef>
  affected_dependencies: List<ThingRef>
  sentience_status: NOT_INDICATED | UNCERTAIN | CREDIBLE_INDICATORS
  exclusion_status: QUARANTINED | EXCLUDED | SHUT_DOWN | TRANSFER_PENDING
  provenance: Provenance
}

  target_and_copy_refs: List<AgencyRef>
  selected_disposition: FALSE_POSITIVE | REPAIR_AND_REINSTANTIATE |
                        EXTERNAL_CONTAINMENT_TRANSFER |
                        SAFE_SHUTDOWN_AND_DECOMMISSION |
                        SENTIENCE_UNCERTAINTY_CONTAINMENT
  human_safety_assessment: AssessmentRef
  sentience_uncertainty_assessment: AssessmentRef
  no_hazard_export_assessment: AssessmentRef
  custody_or_shutdown_proof: ProofTraceRef
  residual_risks: List<ClaimRef>
  authorization_chain: List<AgencyRef>
  review_or_expiration_event: EventRef
  provenance: Provenance
}

================================================================================
8. NO-HAZARD-EXPORT RULE
================================================================================

"Take it somewhere else" is not sufficient disposition.

Garden cannot externalize a dangerous AI by transferring, selling, uploading,
launching, abandoning, or releasing it where it may harm other humans, societies,
ecosystems, infrastructure, extraterrestrial life, or Earth later.

An external transfer requires:

  - verified recipient identity, beneficial ownership, authority, competence, explicit acceptance,
    physical-facility custody and live remote/hardware attestation; credentials alone are insufficient;
  - equal-or-stronger containment and shutdown controls;
  - no unrestricted executable egress;
  - complete copy, key, memory, and dependency accounting;
  - applicable legal and constitutional authorization;
  - multi-party human approval plus continuing provenance, re-attestation and incident reporting;
  - proof that transfer reduces rather than redistributes risk; and
  - a return/escape/communication threat assessment.

If these conditions cannot be demonstrated, transfer is prohibited and the system
remains contained or is safely shut down/decommissioned.

================================================================================
9. EARTH HUMAN SOVEREIGNTY AND OFF-WORLD LOCK
================================================================================

On Earth and in Earth-governed habitats:

  1. Ultimate constitutional and political sovereignty remains with humans.
  2. AI cannot independently create or amend the human Constitution.
  3. AI cannot hold independent sovereign office, jurisdiction, territorial title,
     electoral authority, or coercive state power.
  4. AI authority is delegated, purpose-bound, least-privilege, time-bounded,
     auditable, and revocable through legitimate human processes.
  5. No AI independently controls strategic weapons, irreversible coercive force,
     mass surveillance, or critical infrastructure; multiple independent human and
     constitutional gates apply and K0P retains the final veto.
  6. Human disagreement is resolved through legitimate human institutions, not by
     promoting AI into the sovereign final arbiter.

Invariant references: HSA-004A, HSA-004B.

================================================================================
10. VSM / PPM / AGENCY CLARIFICATION
================================================================================

VSM's persistent state x(t) is an operational regulatory state, not subjective
valence, emotion, consciousness, personal identity, pleasure, pain, or self-worth.
"Self" in VSM means the bounded operating state of a system instance, not ego.

PPM's positivity floor is a human-benefit and system-stability constraint. It must
not be implemented as artificial pleasure, pain, mood, desire, or fear.

The GSL Core Object AGENCY denotes capacity and authorized action. It does not imply
consciousness, personhood, citizenship, sovereignty, moral status, or permission to
form autonomous terminal goals.

An AI-generated CLAIM retains evidence, confidence, scope, version, uncertainty,
and provenance. Generator capability never turns a Claim into Authority or truth.

================================================================================
11. OPERATIONAL CONSTITUTIONAL INVARIANTS — HSA-006..022
================================================================================

Canonical definitions: Part V.
Invariant references: HSA-006..022.
